● Agent "Research Mnestic and SeekStorm" finished · 4m 49s

● Both reports are in, and the first thing worth saying is that one of your
  premises needs adjusting before any architecture follows from it.

  What these two actually are

  Mnestic is not an agent-memory framework. It's a database — a maintained fork
  of CozoDB (v0.13.1, MPL-2.0, forked from upstream at Dec 2024 after upstream
  went quiet). Relational-graph-vector, queried in Datalog. Crucially, the crate
  is mnestic but the import name is cozo — deliberate drop-in compatibility. It
  ships HNSW vector indexes, BM25 full-text, MinHash-LSH dedup, graph
  algorithms, and bitemporal time travel. It is synchronous — no tokio; you'd
  call it under spawn_blocking. One maintainer, 35 stars, 559 downloads, 21
  releases in two months, and an explicit non-promise of API or storage-format
  stability.

  SeekStorm (v3.3.1, Apache-2.0, 1.9k stars, actively released) is a search
  engine: BM25F + proximity, phrase/boolean, facets and numeric-range filters,
  per-field boost, KWIC snippets, typo tolerance, autocomplete — and native ANN
  vector search with RRF hybrid fusion. Fully async/tokio-native, IndexArc = 
  Arc<RwLock<Index>>, Send+Sync.

  So they overlap on the two things you'd most want from a memory system: BM25 
  and vector search. That's the central question this scoping has to answer, and
  it isn't answerable from the libraries — only from what you want memory to
  do.

  The four decisive facts

  1. The embeddings gap. Mnestic's HNSW indexes vectors you supply — it has no
  embedding pipeline and calls no API. artist has no embedding provider wired
  (the only hit in the whole workspace is a comment in
  llm-provider/src/registry.rs:3 noting Voyage is embedding-only), and the Codex
  subscription proxy won't give you one. So "semantic memory on Mnestic"
  implies adding an embedding dependency — a local model or a second API key.
  SeekStorm sidesteps this entirely with in-process model2vec-rs static
  embeddings. That asymmetry is real and load-bearing.

  2. SeekStorm's delete story is bad for a corpus that churns. Deletes take
  effect immediately but reclaim no space and do not recompute BM25 scores; the
  docs literally recommend reindexing over deleting. Memories get corrected and
  superseded constantly. Mnestic, being a transactional DB with bitemporal
  validity, handles supersession natively — that's arguably its strongest
  argument here.

  3. Bitemporality resonates with what you already built. artist's sessions are
  event-sourced with rewind-as-masking, and provider.context.v1 is already a
  rewind-masked opaque sidecar. Mnestic's valid-time/transaction-time :as_of
  reads are the same idea in a query language — "what did I believe about this
  project as of seq N" becomes expressible rather than hand-rolled.

  4. artist already has every injection point you'd need, all four load-bearing 
  and tested. Nothing here requires new plumbing:

  ┌─────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┬───────────────────┐
  │       Channel       │             Site              │     Character     │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Static block in     │ resources/mod.rs:72           │ Cache-stable,     │
  │ preamble            │ prompt_section()              │ always-on, costs  │
  │                     │                               │ tokens every call │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Prompt-conditioned  │ lib.rs:594-603 (skill         │ Retrieval keyed   │
  │ prepend             │ sections)                     │ to the user's     │
  │                     │                               │ text              │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │                     │                               │ Survives          │
  │ Out-of-history      │ ttsr.rs:225-246               │ compaction and    │
  │ injection           │ RequestPatch::extra_context   │ handoff by        │
  │                     │                               │ construction      │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │                     │ find.rs shape, registered     │ Zero idle cost,   │
  │ Model-pull tool     │ lib.rs:675-687                │ model decides     │
  │                     │                               │ when              │
  └─────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┴───────────────────┘

  And TodoStore (todo.rs:1-10) is the exact template for harness-owned state
  that crosses a context wipe verbatim — its doc comment already argues the
  case: "everything else crossing a handoff boundary is lossy prose."

  The tension to keep in view: you spent real effort making the preamble a
  stable prompt-cache prefix (skills go on the user turn, not the preamble, for
  exactly this reason). Any per-turn retrieval that varies the prefix spends
  some of that back.

  Let me record the option map, then put the decisive choices to you.

● Confirmed, and it settles the conflict with a caveat worth knowing: rig-core 
  0.41 ships providers/voyageai.rs with a VOYAGE_CODE_3 constant and the 
  rerank-2.5 models — that's why your own registry.rs:3 comment says Voyage is
  "embedding-only," it was excluded from ProviderKind precisely because rig's
  Voyage integration has no completion model. So hosted embeddings cost zero new
  dependencies.

  The caveat: rig's Voyage request body sends documents only — no input_type, no
  output_dimension, no output_dtype. Voyage's docs are explicit that omitting
  input_type (query vs document asymmetry) measurably degrades retrieval. So
  "free via rig" means voyage-code-3 in its default 1024-dim float
  configuration, with no Matryoshka truncation and no binary quantization,
  unless you hand-roll the client.

  Embedding option space

  First, the structural finding: your two corpora want opposite things. Facts
  are ~5k items that barely churn — quality wins, throughput is irrelevant. Code
  is ~40k chunks that churn constantly — re-index throughput dominates, and
  it's the axis where a wrong choice makes the system unusable rather than
  merely worse.

  Scorecards

  Quality numbers come from three mutually incomparable scales; I've labeled
  each and won't mix them.

  Stack
  A. Lexical only: SeekStorm BM25F / FTS5
  B. Static local + BM25: potion-code-16M-v2 + BM25, RRF — SeekStorm
  Inference::Model2Vec
  C. Local transformer: fastembed 5.17.4 + CodeRankEmbed (int8 ONNX)
  D. Ollama sidecar: embeddinggemma 622 MB / nomic-embed-text 274 MB
  E. Hosted voyage: rig voyageai.rs → voyage-code-3
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Code quality
  A. Lexical only: CoIR 29.8 (paper) / 42.3 (MinishLab)
  B. Static local + BM25: potion alone 39.1 CoIR — below BM25. Hybrid: 0.854 
  NDCG@10 (vendor bench)
  C. Local transformer: CodeRankEmbed 60.1 CoIR, CSN MRR 77.9
  D. Ollama sidecar: ~73.5% (Jina 25-bench) for Qwen3-0.6B
  E. Hosted voyage: 79.2% (Jina 25-bench), +13.8% vs OpenAI-3-large over 32 code

  datasets
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Re-index
  A. Lexical only: instant
  B. Static local + BM25: 8,000/sec → 40k chunks in ~5 s
  C. Local transformer: ~50/sec/vCPU → 40k in ~2 min on 10 cores
  D. Ollama sidecar: unmeasured; vendors retracted claims
  E. Hosted voyage: 3M TPM → 16M tok in ~5.4 min
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Query latency
  A. Lexical only: <2 ms
  B. Static local + BM25: ~1.5 ms
  C. Local transformer: ~20–50 ms
  D. Ollama sidecar: 15–50 ms localhost
  E. Hosted voyage: 50–150 ms network ⚠️ interactive path
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Privacy
  A. Lexical only: ✅ local
  B. Static local + BM25: ✅ local
  C. Local transformer: ✅ local
  D. Ollama sidecar: ✅ localhost
  E. Hosted voyage: ❌ code leaves the machine
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Deps
  A. Lexical only: none new
  B. Static local + BM25: model2vec-rs (MIT), pure Rust, fancy-regex feature
  avoids onig
  C. Local transformer: ort 2.0.0-rc.13 — still RC; downloads ORT binaries at
  build
  D. Ollama sidecar: daemon must run; breaks single-binary for other users
  E. Hosted voyage: zero — already vendored
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  RAM/disk here
  A. Lexical only: ~0
  B. Static local + BM25: model 30 MB; index 40k×256d = 41 MB
  C. Local transformer: model ~140 MB int8; index 40k×768d = 123 MB
  D. Ollama sidecar: 274–622 MB resident
  E. Hosted voyage: index 40k×1024d = 164 MB
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Cost
  A. Lexical only: $0
  B. Static local + BM25: $0
  C. Local transformer: $0
  D. Ollama sidecar: $0
  E. Hosted voyage: $0 for ~8 months (200M free), then $4.35/mo
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  artist effort
  A. Lexical only: low
  B. Static local + BM25: medium
  C. Local transformer: medium-high
  D. Ollama sidecar: low-medium
  E. Hosted voyage: lowest

  Three things I'd flag as load-bearing:

  The Semble result is the strongest evidence in the whole pile, and also the 
  shakiest. MinishLab's Semble fuses potion-code-16M-v2 with BM25 via RRF over
  tree-sitter chunks and reports 99% of CodeRankEmbed-hybrid quality at 218×
  faster indexing, ~250 ms per repo. SeekStorm ≥3.1.0 exposes exactly that
  combination in-process in Rust. But the 0.854 is MinishLab's own benchmark,
  hybrid-vs-hybrid, and on the neutral CoIR scale potion-code alone scores below
  plain BM25. The honest reading: static embeddings are not good, but fusion 
  with BM25 recovers most of the gap — which is a claim about the architecture,
  not the model.

  Your hardware constrains this more than cost does. 15 GiB RAM with 7.4 GiB of
  swap already in use, no usable GPU, 37 GB disk at 92% full. That rules out the
  1.5B/7B open code models outright and makes a resident 622 MB daemon a real
  tax. It does not rule out CodeRankEmbed int8 (~140 MB, MIT, and the best local
  quality by a wide margin on the neutral benchmark).

  Cost is genuinely not a decision input. Your entire first month is ~40.7M
  tokens — $8.13 at the most expensive credible rate. The 200M free allowance
  covers ~8 months. If you reject hosted, reject it for latency or privacy, not
  price.

  Let me record this and put the remaining decisions to you.

● Your Canvas notes change the weighting here: "Pure Rust, no Node" — driven by
  the README's premise that TS-managed harnesses are why Artist exists — is a
  prior that cuts straight across this scorecard. Applied to embeddings it
  argues against Ollama (daemon), against ort 2.0.0-rc.13 (C++ ONNX Runtime,
  downloads prebuilt binaries at build time), and hard against llama-cpp-2
  (cmake + C++ on every builder's machine). It argues for model2vec-rs (pure
  Rust, and its fancy-regex feature drops even the onig C dep).

  It also promotes an option I'd otherwise have listed as a footnote: rten, a
  pure-Rust ONNX engine with no ONNX Runtime and no C++, supporting int8
  quantization. It's the only candidate that is simultaneously pure-Rust,
  dependency-free, and a real transformer. If it runs CodeRankEmbed int8, it
  collapses the entire local tradeoff. I could not verify its BERT coverage or
  throughput — that's a spike, not a fact.

● Agent "Research ast-bro and code chunking" finished · 7m 27s

● Correction to something I told you earlier, since it changes a decision input:
  artist already requires a C toolchain. hashline-tools declares tree-sitter = 
  "0.26" and tree-sitter-rust = "0.24" directly (and it's a path dep of
  artist-tools), alongside bundled SQLite, ring, aws-lc-sys, zstd-sys and cc 
  1.4.0 in the lock. So the Canvas "pure Rust" prior is about no Node/TS, not
  about no C. The real objection to fastembed is narrower than I framed it — not
  "it's C," but that ort is still a release candidate that downloads prebuilt
  binaries at build time.

  On ast-bro: it's real (MIT, Rust, aeroxy/ast-bro v3.0.0, 218 stars, actively
  pushed yesterday) — but you cannot use it as a library. Every module in its
  lib.rs is private; the entire public surface is run() and LineRange. It's also
  3 months old with 369 lifetime downloads and one author.

  Two things worth knowing about it: its chunker is ~350 lines of MIT code in
  src/search/chunker.rs that you could legitimately vendor (AST split at
  top-level declarations, greedy-pack to 1500 chars, never splits
  mid-declaration, but carries no symbol name or node kind). And its retrieval
  design is a Rust reimplementation of MinishLab's semble — potion-code-16M 
  static embeddings + BM25, which is precisely the option you declined in favour
  of rten/CodeRankEmbed.

● Agent "Deep-dive rten feasibility" finished · 10m 46s

● Agent "Deep-dive Mnestic/Cozo API" finished · 12m 45s

● Agent "Deep-dive rten feasibility" finished · 14m 4s

● All research is in. One finding materially undercuts the reason "one store —
  Mnestic" looked attractive, so you should see it before I finalize.

  Mnestic's bitemporality and its search indexes are mutually exclusive on the 
  same relation. In released 0.13.1, relation.rs:947 reject_txtime_relation() is
  called from ::hnsw create, ::fts create, ::lsh create, ::index create and
  ::set_triggers — a TxTime relation can carry no index or trigger at all. And
  search atoms don't accept a validity clause (cozoscript.pest:103), so there is
  no as-of vector or FTS search. You can have time travel, or you can have
  retrieval, not both on one relation.

  The workaround is sound but plainer than the pitch: keep facts on a normal
  relation with live: Bool + superseded_by: Uuid?, and create the HNSW index
  with a create-time filter: live so superseded rows leave the index
  automatically. That works well — but note it reduces supersession to roughly
  what any engine would have given you, which was the main differentiator over
  the alternatives.

  Three more verified gotchas worth having on record:

  - HNSW filter: and radius: are post-filters over the ef candidate pool, not
  in-search filters — hnsw_knn never passes filter bytecode into
  hnsw_search_level. This directly contradicts the CozoDB docs. A selective
  filter silently returns fewer than k rows. Create-time filters are the fix.
  - The RocksDB backend commits without syncing its WAL, and the
  set_durable_writes opt-in does not exist in 0.13.1 — it's unreleased work on
  main. Same for ::warnings.
  - Good news: RRF and MMR are real built-in fixed rules (unconditional, not
  behind graph-algo), plus a HybridSearch Rust builder that generates the
  standard fusion script. And mnestic's FTS defaults to genuine BM25 with k1/b
  knobs, unlike upstream's tf-idf.

  On rten the verdict is GO, gated on a ~4–6 hour spike. All 29 op types in the
  fp32 CodeRankEmbed export are covered by rten 0.24.0's 130-op registry; rten
  loads .onnx directly since v0.23 so there's no Python or rten-convert in the
  build; and rten-text covers WordPiece/BertNormalizer, letting you skip HF
  tokenizers entirely (whose defaults pull both onig C and esaxx-rs C++). The
  real risks are that rten has bus factor 1 with no release in 7 months, a known
  BERT optimizer miscompile (#1381) fixed only on main, and zero published 
  encoder throughput numbers anywhere.

  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~/.claude/plans/federated-puzzling-sun.md · /plan to edit
     Artist memory system — Mnestic + rten

     Context

     Artist has no memory. A grep for embedding|vector|retriev|semantic across
     crates/ returns one unrelated comment. Every session starts cold:
     preferences
     you have already stated, decisions already made and their rationale, and
     knowledge of the codebase all have to be re-derived from files and
     re-explained
     by hand. The event-sourced session store (crates/artist-session) already
     keeps
     everything that ever happened, but nothing reads it back.

     This builds two corpora with one substrate:

     - Facts — durable preferences, project constraints, decisions + rationale,
     corrections. ~5k items, curated, superseded rather than appended.
     - Code — an AST-chunked, embedded index of the repository. ~40k chunks,
     constant churn.

     Explicitly out of scope: indexing the session archive as a searchable
     corpus,
     and cross-compaction working memory (the TodoStore already covers the
     latter
     pattern for the one case that needed it).

     The intended outcome: the agent recalls what you told it three sessions
     ago,
     finds code by what it does rather than what it's called, and writes new
     memories at the moments that actually carry signal — without you managing
     any
     of it.

     Decisions taken

     ┌────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┐
     │    Axis    │                          Decision
     │
     ├────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ Substrate  │ Mnestic 0.13.1 (crate mnestic, imports as cozo), RocksDB
     │
     │            │ backend
     │
     ├────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ Embeddings │ rten (pure-Rust ONNX) + CodeRankEmbed int8/fp32, local, no
     │
     │            │ egress
     │
     ├────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ Chunking   │ text-splitter code feature over tree-sitter grammars
     │
     ├────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ Write path │ memory tool + three automatic triggers (below)
     │
     ├────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ Read path  │ model-pull tool + prompt-conditioned prepend +
     │
     │            │ output-triggered injection
     │
     ├────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ Scope      │ one continuous build to completion — not staged increments
     │
     └────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┘

     SeekStorm is not used: Mnestic carries BM25 and HNSW natively, so a second
     engine would only duplicate them and add a two-index sync hazard.

     Verified constraints that shape the design

     These were checked against published source, not documentation, and several
     contradict the docs. Getting them wrong silently degrades retrieval.

     1. TxTime relations reject every index and trigger.
     relation.rs:947 reject_txtime_relation() is called from ::hnsw create,
     ::fts create, ::lsh create, ::index create, ::set_triggers. Bitemporality
     and search are mutually exclusive on one relation. There is also no as-of
     vector/FTS search — search atoms take no validity clause
     (cozoscript.pest:103). → Searchable facts use live: Bool +
     superseded_by; a non-indexed TxTime sibling carries the audit trail.
     2. HNSW filter:/radius: are post-filters over the ef pool, not
     in-search filters (hnsw.rs:1196 never passes filter bytecode into
     hnsw_search_level). The CozoDB docs claim otherwise. A selective filter
     silently returns fewer than k rows. → Use create-time filter:, and
     set ef to a generous multiple of k.
     3. RocksDB commits without syncing its WAL in 0.13.1, and
     set_durable_writes
     does not exist in the released crate (unreleased on main). Same for
     ::warnings. → Treat the store as a rebuildable projection; JSONL session
     events are canonical truth (§4).
     4. No literal vector syntax. Pass Vec<f32> as a DataValue::List of f64
     and convert in-script with vec($qv), or build
     DataValue::Vec(Vector::F32(..))
     directly to avoid the f32→f64→f32 round-trip.
     5. Use Int surrogate keys on any HNSW-indexed relation. The index embeds
     the base key twice (fr_/to_); at 40k rows / m:16 that is ~1.37M index
     rows — ~115 MB with Int keys vs ~205 MB with 40-char String keys.
     6. mnestic FTS defaults to real BM25 with k1/b knobs (upstream Cozo
     defaults to tf-idf and has no BM25). RRF and MMR are built-in fixed rules,
     registered unconditionally — not gated behind graph-algo.
     7. run_script is blocking. DbInstance is Send + Clone over Arcs.
     Wrap every call in spawn_blocking — required anyway by the standing rule
     that nothing blocks the TUI input loop.
     8. HNSW index relation columns are fr__sub_idx/to__sub_idx, not
     fr__field_idx/to__field_idx as docs.cozodb.org shows.
     9. ::reindex uses the slow incremental path and holds the relation write
     lock
     for minutes. For a full rebuild prefer ::hnsw drop + ::hnsw create.

     Architecture

                       artist-cli / artist-agent
                                 |
                         artist-memory (new crate)
                                 |
           +---------------------+---------------------+
           |                     |                     |
       MemoryStore          Embedder              Chunker
       (Mnestic/cozo)       (rten +               (text-splitter
        spawn_blocking       CodeRankEmbed)        + tree-sitter)
           |
       global.rocks  <config_root>/memory/
       project.rocks <config_root>/tools/<project-hash>/

     Two stores, not one. Global facts (preferences that follow you between
     repos) and project data live in separate databases. This mirrors
     agents::discover's global-then-project layering
     (artist-agent/src/resources/agents.rs:23-38), and is additionally forced by
     constraint 2: partitioning one index by a scope filter would hit the
     post-filter path and silently drop results.

     New crate: crates/artist-memory

     Add to Cargo.toml workspace members. Depends on artist-session (events,
     Recorder), rten, rten-text, text-splitter, mnestic, regex, tokio.

     mnestic = { version = "=0.13.1", default-features = false,
                 features = ["storage-rocksdb", "graph-algo"] }

     Pin exactly and commit the lockfile — the crate makes no storage-format
     stability promise, and RocksDB asserts storage_version == 3 at open and
     panics on mismatch. default-features = false drops the requests/minreq
     HTTP feature, which only powers URL fetching in CsvReader/JsonReader and is
     useless here. graph-algo is kept for BudgetedTraversal over fact relations.

     1. Schema

     Applied idempotently at open, guarded by a _meta {key => value} relation
     holding a schema version.

     cozoscript
     :create fact {
         fact_id: Int
         =>
         subject: String, predicate: String, object: String,
         text: String,
         emb: <F32; 768>,
         live: Bool default true,
         superseded_by: Int? default null,
         source_session: String, source_seq: Int,
         origin: String,                    -- which write point produced it
         created_at: Float default now(),
     }

     :create fact_history { fact_id: Int, tt: TxTime => text: String, live: Bool
     }

     :create rel { src: Int, kind: String, dst: Int => weight: Float default 1.0
     }

     :create chunk {
         chunk_id: Int
         =>
         path: String, lang: String,
         start_line: Int, end_line: Int,
         body: String,
         emb: <F32; 768>,
         content_hash: String, mtime: Float,
     }

     ::hnsw create fact:emb_idx {
         dim: 768, dtype: F32, fields: [emb], distance: Cosine,
         m: 16, ef_construction: 200,
         filter: live,                      -- create-time; see constraint 2
     }
     ::fts create fact:text_fts {
         extractor: text, tokenizer: Simple,
         filters: [Lowercase, Stemmer('English'), Stopwords('en')]
     }
     ::lsh create fact:dedupe {
         extractor: text, tokenizer: NGram, n_gram: 5,
         n_perm: 200, target_threshold: 0.8,
     }

     ::hnsw create chunk:emb_idx {
         dim: 768, dtype: F32, fields: [emb], distance: Cosine,
         m: 16, ef_construction: 200,
     }
     ::fts create chunk:body_fts {
         extractor: body, tokenizer: Simple,
         filters: [Lowercase, Stemmer('English')]
     }

     fact_history carries no index (constraint 1) and exists purely as the
     audit trail: ::history, ::history_gc, ::evict work on it.

     Supersession is :put the new fact + :update the old one to live = false, 
     superseded_by = <new_id>, plus a fact_history append — one
     multi_transaction. The create-time filter: live removes it from vector
     recall automatically.

     Before every :put of a new fact, probe fact:dedupe — MinHash-LSH over
     5-grams catches near-duplicate wording cheaply. Semantic duplicates with
     different wording are caught by an HNSW distance threshold on the same
     path.

     2. Embeddings (artist-memory/src/embed.rs)

     rten loads .onnx directly since v0.23 — no Python, no rten-convert in
     the build. rten-text provides WordPiece + BertNormalizer + BertPreTokenizer
     - automatic [CLS]/[SEP], which exactly matches CodeRankEmbed's tokenizer
     and
     lets us skip the HF tokenizers crate entirely (whose defaults pull onig C
     and esaxx-rs C++).

     Model: jamie8johnson/CodeRankEmbed-onnx (MIT, fp32) or the int8 export.
     Graph inputs are input_ids + attention_mask only (no token_type_ids);
     outputs token_embeddings and sentence_embedding — CLS pooling is in-graph,
     L2 normalization is not, so normalize in Rust. Queries must be prefixed
     Represent this query for searching relevant code: ; documents must not be.

     Model is Send + Sync and run(&self, ..) takes &self — hold one
     Arc<Model>. rten runs its own internal rayon pool (separate from the global
     one), so a single spawn_blocking call already fans out across cores; do not
     issue many concurrent inference tasks. Model file is downloaded once to
     <config_root>/models/ and cached.

     Blocking gate: the rten spike (§9) runs before this module is written.
     On NO-GO, fall back to BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5 on rten (384-dim — adjust the
     schema dim), which is still pure Rust. Do not fall back to candle: 0.10.0+
     has a non-optional tokenizers/onig dep, which forfeits the property that
     motivated rten.

     3. Chunking (artist-memory/src/chunk.rs)

     text-splitter 0.32 (MIT) with the code feature — the only maintained
     purpose-built option with real token-budget awareness:

     let splitter = CodeSplitter::new(tree_sitter_rust::LANGUAGE, 1000)?;
     for (offset, text) in splitter.chunk_indices(source) { .. }

     tree-sitter 0.26 and tree-sitter-rust 0.24 are already direct
     dependencies of hashline-tools, so this adds no new toolchain requirement.
     Add grammars behind cargo features, defaulting to Rust, TS/JS, Python, Go
     and
     Markdown — each grammar costs roughly 0.5–5 MB of incompressible binary.

     text-splitter yields byte offsets only; convert to 1-indexed line ranges
     with
     a newline count over the prefix, and attach path/lang. Files with no
     grammar fall back to blank-line + brace-depth heuristic chunking so no
     language
     is unindexable.

     Do not depend on ast-bro: every module in its lib.rs is private (the
     public surface is run() and LineRange), so it is unusable as a library.

     Incremental reindex. Workspace::open already runs an fff-search file
     watcher (artist-tools/src/workspace.rs:37-50). Hook that signal: on file
     change, re-chunk, compare content_hash per chunk, and :put/:rm only what
     moved. Full index only on first run or a schema version bump.

     4. Durability: JSONL is truth, Mnestic is a projection

     Given constraint 3, the store must be reconstructible. Add one event kind
     (artist-session/src/event.rs:110-146, one struct + one event_kinds! line +
     a pub use; unknown kinds already degrade to SessionEvent::Unknown, so older
     binaries tolerate it):

     pub struct MemoryWritten {
         pub fact_id: i64, pub text: String, pub subject: String,
         pub predicate: String, pub object: String,
         pub origin: String, pub superseded: Option<i64>,
     }

     MemoryStore::restore(&events) replays over visible_events exactly as
     TodoStore::restore does (artist-agent/src/todo.rs:39-45) — so memory is
     rewind-aware for free. Code chunks need no events; they are derived from
     files.

     Ship artist memory export|import|rebuild|verify subcommands wrapping
     export_relations / import_relations (Rust APIs — there are no ::export
     sysops). After any import, run ::reindex, because both bulk paths bypass
     index
     maintenance and only warn via log::warn!. Pin =0.13.1; export before any
     version bump.

     5. Write paths

     The memory tool

     A PortableTool following artist-tools/src/find.rs, registered in the
     built-in array at artist-agent/src/lib.rs:675-687 so profile.permits gates
     it. Modes: store, search, supersede, forget. Per
     tool_prompt.rs:1-8, the tool description is the only model-facing guidance
     channel — usage rules live there, not in the system prompt.

     Automatic trigger 1 — user correction / pushback

     A regex::RegexSet prescreen over the user's message text (patterns like
     "no, actually", "don't", "stop doing", "I told you", "instead of"). A hit
     schedules a background extraction pass over the last exchange, which
     returns
     zero or more candidate facts through a judge prompt. This is the
     highest-signal
     trigger: a correction is a durable preference being stated out loud.

     Kept as a local matcher inside artist-memory rather than a new
     MatchTarget on artist-rules — TTSR's targets are all streamed model
     output, and user text is neither streamed nor abortable. Reuse the RegexSet
     pattern (artist-rules/src/matcher.rs:36-65), not the crate's semantics.

     Automatic trigger 2 — model output (TTSR-shaped)

     MemoryHook: AgentHook, observing on_text_delta / on_tool_call_delta with
     a bounded tail window and a RegexSet prescreen — the same hot-path budget
     as
     StreamMatcher (evaluate on ≥64 new bytes or a newline). Fires on the model
     stating a durable decision plus rationale.

     It must never abort. TTSR's fire() already models exactly this: with
     can_abort = false (ttsr.rs:117-119) it records the firing and injection but
     skips the terminate. Memory reuses that shape unconditionally.

     Automatic trigger 3 — git commit boundary

     on_tool_result — the same hook SteeringHook already implements
     (artist-agent/src/steering.rs:69-70). Detect a successful git commit in a
     bash tool result, then extract from the diff plus the exchange that
     produced
     it. No file watcher, no new git module needed (the repo has none — git
     awareness today is only root discovery).

     All three triggers are fire-and-forget onto a background task. None blocks
     the stream, the tool loop, or the input box.

     6. Read paths

     Retrieval query

     One hybrid query per lookup, using Mnestic's built-in fusion. Prefer the
     HybridSearch Rust builder (db.hybrid_search(&q), with
     hybrid_search_script(&q) to inspect what it generates):

     cozoscript
     sem[id, score] := ~fact:emb_idx{ fact_id: id | query: vec($qv), k: 50, ef:
     200,
                                      bind_distance: __d }, score = -__d
     txt[id, score] := ~fact:text_fts{ fact_id: id | query: $qt, k: 50,
     bind_score: score }
     combined[__lid, id, score] := sem[id, score], __lid = 'semantic'
     combined[__lid, id, score] := txt[id, score], __lid = 'text'
     ?[id, score] <~ ReciprocalRankFusion(combined[__lid, id, score], k: 60.0)
     :order -score
     :limit 15

     Note score = -__dist: RRF requires all legs normalized so higher is better.
     Add MaximalMarginalRelevance over the fused set to suppress near-duplicate
     recall. Set ScriptRunOptions::new().with_timeout(5.0) per read and
     set_default_query_timeout(Some(30.0)) at startup — this is the guard
     against a
     pathological recursion hanging the runtime.

     Channel 1 — memory tool (model-pull)

     Zero idle token cost, zero cache disruption. Covered by the tool above.

     Channel 2 — prompt-conditioned prepend

     Retrieve on the user's text and prepend as UserContent::text at index 0 of
     the
     seed prompt, exactly beside explicit_skill_section at
     artist-agent/src/lib.rs:594-603. Deliberately not the preamble: the
     preamble is a stable prompt-cache prefix (skills were moved off it in
     6147286
     for this reason), and per-turn retrieval must not invalidate it.

     Channel 3 — output-triggered injection

     The trigger-2 hook fires a retrieval, and on_completion_call collects
     whatever
     has landed and injects it via RequestPatch::extra_context, reusing
     injection_document's shape (ttsr.rs:196-209, 238-245). Because the model
     is still streaming when the trigger fires, the retrieval has a natural
     window to
     complete before the next completion call — non-blocking by construction.
     This channel also survives compaction and handoff, since it lives outside
     history.

     Every injected block is wrapped in a <memory> element with provenance, and
     is
     user-role, never Message::System (the ChatGPT provider hoists system
     messages
     into instructions, away from the point of use).

     7. Configuration

     Settings carries #[serde(deny_unknown_fields)] at
     artist-cli/src/settings.rs:26 — an unrecognized [memory] table is a hard
     parse error, so the struct must be extended. Follow CompactionSettings
     (settings.rs:42-65) exactly: optional nested table, all-Option fields, an
     is_empty() skip predicate, a resolved MemoryConfig with Default beside
     CompactionConfig (settings.rs:69-83), folded into
     EffectiveSettings::resolve
     (settings.rs:139-189) with the same
     project.x.or(global.x).unwrap_or(default)
     chain.

     Keys: enabled, model, dim, k, ef, auto_write (per-trigger toggles),
     index_globs. Behaviour lives in settings.toml; no API keys are involved at
     all, since embeddings are local.

     8. Sizing (computed, not guessed)

     ┌────────┬────────┬──────────┬──────────────────────────┬────────┐
     │        │  rows  │ vectors  │        HNSW index        │  text  │
     ├────────┼────────┼──────────┼──────────────────────────┼────────┤
     │ chunks │ 40,000 │ 122.9 MB │ ~115 MB (Int keys, m:16) │ ~40 MB │
     ├────────┼────────┼──────────┼──────────────────────────┼────────┤
     │ facts  │ 5,000  │ 15.4 MB  │ ~14 MB                   │ small  │
     └────────┴────────┴──────────┴──────────────────────────┴────────┘

     ≈ 330–400 MB on disk before compression; run ::compact after bulk load.
     The HNSW index lives on disk, not in RAM (it is a normal stored relation) —
     which matters given this machine has 15 GiB RAM with swap already in use.
     Model
     adds ~140 MB (int8) or ~548 MB (fp32) resident. Disk is 37 GB free.

     Initial index build: expect minutes, dominated by writing ~1.37M index
     tuples in one transaction. MNESTIC_INDEX_BUILD_THREADS controls
     parallelism.

     9. Verification

     The rten spike runs first and gates §2. Use rten-cli before writing Rust:

     1. Load and run jamie8johnson/CodeRankEmbed-onnx fp32. All 29 op types in
     that
     export are in rten 0.24.0's 130-op registry, so this should pass.
     2. Re-run with --no-optimize. Issue #1381 is a BERT-shaped optimizer
     miscompile fixed only after 0.24.0; if outputs differ, disable optimization
     or pin a git rev.
     3. Numerically validate against sentence-transformers on ~50 code snippets
     (cosine agreement). Non-negotiable — this is what catches the Einsum and
     If-subgraph risks, four Einsum fixes having landed post-release.
     4. Try the int8 export; on failure (rten issue #596, u8×u8 MatMulInteger)
     fall
     back to fp32 or self-quantize with MatMulConstBOnly=True.
     5. Time 512-token embeddings — no encoder throughput figures for rten exist
     anywhere, so this is the first real measurement.

     Then, per subsystem:

     - Store: unit tests for schema apply/idempotency, supersession lifecycle
     (assert the superseded fact leaves HNSW recall), LSH dedupe admission, and
     an
     export → drop → import → ::reindex → identical-results round trip.
     - Rewind: append memory events, rewind past them, assert
     MemoryStore::restore over visible_events matches — same property
     TodoStore is tested for.
     - Chunker: property test that chunks tile the file with no gaps or overlaps
     and that line ranges round-trip to byte offsets.
     - Retrieval: a fixture repo with known answers; assert RRF beats either leg
     alone on recall@10. Verify a create-time-filtered query returns k results
     (guarding constraint 2).
     - Hooks: assert the memory hook never returns a terminating Flow, and
     that a slow retrieval does not delay stream delivery.
     - End-to-end: cargo test --release (the workspace runs tests against the
     release profile per 671b89e), then drive the real TUI — state a preference,
     start a fresh session, confirm it is recalled; ask a conceptual code
     question
     and confirm the right chunk surfaces; make a commit and confirm a fact is
     written.

     10. Documentation

     Rewrite the relevant sections of docs/architecture.md (settings layering at
     :346-366, event kinds — which already lag the code) and add a memory
     section.
     docs/profiles.md invariant 7 ("todo state is owned by the harness … and
     survives a context wipe without summarization") is the precedent this
     system
     extends; cite and generalize it.

     Staging

     Per [[gortnite-branch-only]], all work lands directly on Gortnite. No side
     branches.

        1 -//! placeholder                                               
        2 -pub struct Embedder;                                          
        1 +//! Local embeddings via rten (pure-Rust ONNX) and CodeRankEmb
          +ed.                                                           
        2 +//!                                                           
        3 +//! Measured properties of this model on CPU, which drive the 
          +choices below:                                                
        4 +//!                                                           
        5 +//! * The graph declares int64 inputs but rten narrows them to
          + **i32**, and its                                             
        6 +//!   readers reject I64 outright — so tensors are built as `i
          +32`.                                                          
        7 +//! * `sentence_embedding` is **CLS-pooled inside the graph** 
          +but **not                                                     
        8 +//!   L2-normalized**, so normalization happens here.         
        9 +//! * Throughput is ~6-7 sequences/sec at batch 32 with peak R
          +SS ~1.1 GB. Batch                                             
       10 +//!   is kept smaller by default to bound memory; a full repos
          +itory index is a                                              
       11 +//!   background job, never a blocking startup step.          
       12 +//! * The published int8 export measured 0.909 mean cosine aga
          +inst fp32 and                                                 
       13 +//!   moved 14% of top-1 results, so fp32 is what we load.    
       14 +                                                              
       15 +use anyhow::{Context, Result, anyhow};                        
       16 +use rten::{Model, NodeId};                                    
       17 +use rten_tensor::NdTensor;                                    
       18 +use rten_tensor::prelude::*;                                  
       19 +use rten_text::tokenizer::{EncodeOptions, Tokenizer};         
       20 +use std::path::Path;                                          
       21 +use std::sync::Arc;                                           
       22 +                                                              
       23 +/// CodeRankEmbed requires this prefix on *queries* only. Omit
          +ting it, or                                                   
       24 +/// applying it to documents, measurably degrades retrieval.  
       25 +pub const QUERY_PREFIX: &str = "Represent this query for searc
          +hing relevant code: ";                                        
       26 +                                                              
       27 +/// Tokens per sequence. Longer inputs are truncated by the to
          +kenizer.                                                      
       28 +pub const MAX_TOKENS: usize = 256;                            
       29 +                                                              
       30 +/// Sequences per forward pass. Small enough to bound peak RSS
          + on a machine                                                 
       31 +/// under memory pressure; throughput is near-flat between 8 a
          +nd 32.                                                        
       32 +pub const BATCH: usize = 8;                                   
       33 +                                                              
       34 +#[derive(Clone)]                                              
       35 +pub struct Embedder {                                         
       36 +    inner: Arc<Inner>,                                        
       37 +}                                                             
       38 +                                                              
       39 +struct Inner {                                                
       40 +    model: Model,                                             
       41 +    tokenizer: Tokenizer,                                     
       42 +    input_ids: NodeId,                                        
       43 +    attention_mask: NodeId,                                   
       44 +    sentence_embedding: NodeId,                               
       45 +    dim: usize,                                               
       46 +}                                                             
       47 +                                                              
       48 +impl Embedder {                                               
       49 +    /// Load a model directory containing `model.onnx` and `to
          +kenizer.json`.                                                
       50 +    ///                                                       
       51 +    /// rten has loaded `.onnx` directly since v0.23, so there
          + is no conversion                                             
       52 +    /// step and no Python anywhere in the build.             
       53 +    pub async fn load(dir: impl AsRef<Path>, dim: usize) -> Re
          +sult<Self> {                                                  
       54 +        let dir = dir.as_ref().to_path_buf();                 
       55 +        tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || Self::load_blockin
          +g(&dir, dim))                                                 
       56 +            .await                                            
       57 +            .map_err(|e| anyhow!("embedder load panicked: {e}"
          +))?                                                           
       58 +    }                                                         
       59 +                                                              
       60 +    fn load_blocking(dir: &Path, dim: usize) -> Result<Self> {
       61 +        let model_path = dir.join("model.onnx");              
       62 +        let tokenizer_path = dir.join("tokenizer.json");      
       63 +        let model = Model::load_file(&model_path)             
       64 +            .map_err(|e| anyhow!("loading {}: {e}", model_path
          +.display()))?;                                                
       65 +        let tokenizer = Tokenizer::from_file(&tokenizer_path) 
       66 +            .map_err(|e| anyhow!("loading {}: {e:?}", tokenize
          +r_path.display()))?;                                          
       67 +                                                              
       68 +        let node = |name: &str| {                             
       69 +            model                                             
       70 +                .find_node(name)                              
       71 +                .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("model has no node name
          +d {name}"))                                                   
       72 +        };                                                    
       73 +        Ok(Self {                                             
       74 +            inner: Arc::new(Inner {                           
       75 +                input_ids: node("input_ids")?,                
       76 +                attention_mask: node("attention_mask")?,      
       77 +                sentence_embedding: node("sentence_embedding")
          +?,                                                            
       78 +                model,                                        
       79 +                tokenizer,                                    
       80 +                dim,                                          
       81 +            }),                                               
       82 +        })                                                    
       83 +    }                                                         
       84 +                                                              
       85 +    pub fn dim(&self) -> usize {                              
       86 +        self.inner.dim                                        
       87 +    }                                                         
       88 +                                                              
       89 +    /// Embed documents. Order is preserved.                  
       90 +    pub async fn embed_documents(&self, texts: Vec<String>) ->
          + Result<Vec<Vec<f32>>> {                                      
       91 +        self.embed(texts, false).await                        
       92 +    }                                                         
       93 +                                                              
       94 +    /// Embed a single query, applying the required instructio
          +n prefix.                                                     
       95 +    pub async fn embed_query(&self, text: &str) -> Result<Vec<
          +f32>> {                                                       
       96 +        let mut out = self.embed(vec![text.to_owned()], true).
          +await?;                                                       
       97 +        out.pop()                                             
       98 +            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("embedder returned no rows 
          +for a query"))                                                
       99 +    }                                                         
      100 +                                                              
      101 +    async fn embed(&self, texts: Vec<String>, is_query: bool) 
          +-> Result<Vec<Vec<f32>>> {                                    
      102 +        if texts.is_empty() {                                 
      103 +            return Ok(Vec::new());                            
      104 +        }                                                     
      105 +        let inner = Arc::clone(&self.inner);                  
      106 +        // rten runs its own internal rayon pool, so one block
          +ing task already                                              
      107 +        // fans out across cores. Issuing many concurrent infe
          +rences would                                                  
      108 +        // oversubscribe rather than speed anything up.       
      109 +        tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || inner.embed_blocki
          +ng(&texts, is_query))                                         
      110 +            .await                                            
      111 +            .map_err(|e| anyhow!("embedding panicked: {e}"))? 
      112 +    }                                                         
      113 +}                                                             
      114 +                                                              
      115 +impl Inner {                                                  
      116 +    fn embed_blocking(&self, texts: &[String], is_query: bool)
          + -> Result<Vec<Vec<f32>>> {                                   
      117 +        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(texts.len());        
      118 +        for batch in texts.chunks(BATCH) {                    
      119 +            out.extend(self.forward(batch, is_query)?);       
      120 +        }                                                     
      121 +        Ok(out)                                               
      122 +    }                                                         
      123 +                                                              
      124 +    fn forward(&self, batch: &[String], is_query: bool) -> Res
          +ult<Vec<Vec<f32>>> {                                          
      125 +        let options = EncodeOptions {                         
      126 +            max_chunk_len: Some(MAX_TOKENS),                  
      127 +            ..Default::default()                              
      128 +        };                                                    
      129 +                                                              
      130 +        let mut encoded = Vec::with_capacity(batch.len());    
      131 +        for text in batch {                                   
      132 +            let prefixed;                                     
      133 +            let input = if is_query {                         
      134 +                prefixed = format!("{QUERY_PREFIX}{text}");   
      135 +                prefixed.as_str()                             
      136 +            } else {                                          
      137 +                text.as_str()                                 
      138 +            };                                                
      139 +            let ids = self                                    
      140 +                .tokenizer                                    
      141 +                .encode(input, Some(options))                 
      142 +                .map_err(|e| anyhow!("tokenizing: {e:?}"))?   
      143 +                .token_ids()                                  
      144 +                .to_vec();                                    
      145 +            encoded.push(ids);                                
      146 +        }                                                     
      147 +                                                              
      148 +        // Pad to the longest sequence in this batch rather th
          +an to MAX_TOKENS:                                             
      149 +        // attention is quadratic in length, so short batches 
          +run much faster.                                              
      150 +        let width = encoded.iter().map(|e| e.len()).max().unwr
          +ap_or(1).max(1);                                              
      151 +        let rows = encoded.len();                             
      152 +                                                              
      153 +        let mut ids = NdTensor::<i32, 2>::zeros([rows, width])
          +;                                                             
      154 +        let mut mask = NdTensor::<i32, 2>::zeros([rows, width]
          +);                                                            
      155 +        for (row, tokens) in encoded.iter().enumerate() {     
      156 +            for (col, token) in tokens.iter().enumerate() {   
      157 +                ids[[row, col]] = *token as i32;              
      158 +                mask[[row, col]] = 1;                         
      159 +            }                                                 
      160 +        }                                                     
      161 +                                                              
      162 +        let outputs = self                                    
      163 +            .model                                            
      164 +            .run(                                             
      165 +                vec![                                         
      166 +                    (self.input_ids, ids.view().into()),      
      167 +                    (self.attention_mask, mask.view().into()),
      168 +                ],                                            
      169 +                &[self.sentence_embedding],                   
      170 +                None,                                         
      171 +            )                                                 
      172 +            .map_err(|e| anyhow!("inference failed: {e}"))?;  
      173 +                                                              
      174 +        let tensor: rten_tensor::Tensor<f32> = outputs        
      175 +            .into_iter()                                      
      176 +            .next()                                           
      177 +            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("model returned no outputs"
          +))?                                                           
      178 +            .try_into()                                       
      179 +            .context("sentence_embedding was not an f32 tensor
          +")?;                                                          
      180 +                                                              
      181 +        let data = tensor.to_vec();                           
      182 +        if data.len() != rows * self.dim {                    
      183 +            return Err(anyhow!(                               
      184 +                "expected {rows}x{} embedding values, got {}",
      185 +                self.dim,                                     
      186 +                data.len()                                    
      187 +            ));                                               
      188 +        }                                                     
      189 +                                                              
      190 +        Ok(data                                               
      191 +            .chunks(self.dim)                                 
      192 +            .map(|row| {                                      
      193 +                // CLS pooling is in-graph; L2 normalization i
          +s not.                                                        
      194 +                let norm = row.iter().map(|v| v * v).sum::<f32
          +>().sqrt();                                                   
      195 +                if norm > 0.0 {                               
      196 +                    row.iter().map(|v| v / norm).collect()    
      197 +                } else {                                      
      198 +                    row.to_vec()                              
      199 +                }                                             
      200 +            })                                                
      201 +            .collect())                                       
      202 +    }                                                         
      203 +}                                                             

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Three issues, one substantive: rten_text::Tokenizer is neither Send nor Sync —
  its internal Box<dyn Model>/Normalizer/PreTokenizer aren't. It can't be
  shared across the blocking pool. The clean fix for a !Send resource in a
  thread pool is a thread-local, which amortizes well since tokio reuses
  blocking threads.

      41 +    /// `rten_text::Tokenizer` is neither `Send` nor `Sync` — i
         +t holds                                                        
      42 +    /// `Box<dyn Model/Normalizer/PreTokenizer>`. So the *sourc
         +e* travels                                                     
      43 +    /// between threads and each blocking worker builds its own
         + copy once, via                                                
      44 +    /// the thread-local below. Tokio reuses blocking threads, 
         +so the parse cost                                              
      45 +    /// is paid a handful of times per process rather than per 
         +call.                                                          
      46 +    tokenizer_json: Arc<str>,                                  
      47      input_ids: NodeId,

      53 +thread_local! {                                                
      54 +    static TOKENIZER: std::cell::RefCell<Option<(usize, Tokeniz
         +er)>> =                                                        
      55 +        const { std::cell::RefCell::new(None) };               
      56 +}                                                              
      57 +                                                               
      58 +/// Run `f` with this thread's tokenizer, building it on first 
         +use. Keyed by                                                  
      59 +/// the source pointer so a differently-configured embedder reb
         +uilds rather                                                   
      60 +/// than silently reusing another model's vocabulary.          
      61 +fn with_tokenizer<T>(json: &Arc<str>, f: impl FnOnce(&Tokenizer
         +) -> Result<T>) -> Result<T> {                                 
      62 +    let key = Arc::as_ptr(json) as *const u8 as usize;         
      63 +    TOKENIZER.with(|cell| {                                    
      64 +        let mut slot = cell.borrow_mut();                      
      65 +        if slot.as_ref().is_none_or(|(k, _)| *k != key) {      
      66 +            let tokenizer = Tokenizer::from_json(json)         
      67 +                .map_err(|e| anyhow!("building tokenizer: {e:?}
         +"))?;                                                          
      68 +            *slot = Some((key, tokenizer));                    
      69 +        }                                                      
      70 +        let (_, tokenizer) = slot.as_ref().expect("tokenizer ju
         +st installed");                                                
      71 +        f(tokenizer)                                           
      72 +    })                                                         
      73 +}

● All 10 tests pass — the artist-session failure was transient, a mid-save from

● Stopping here to report, since a fair amount is now settled and one thing
  needs flagging.

  Heads-up: concurrent work in this repo

  The working tree changed substantially during this session — there's now an
  artist-canvas crate, artist-session/src/ask.rs, and modifications to
  artist-session/src/{memory,convert,event,lib}.rs. At one point artist-session
  didn't compile (memory.rs:69 calls an undefined normalize), which briefly
  broke my build; it resolved on retry, so it was a mid-save. I haven't touched
  any of those files. artist-canvas isn't in the workspace members list yet.

  Spike verdict: GO, fp32 — with two findings that changed the plan

  rten runs CodeRankEmbed correctly, and is ~2.5× faster than ONNX Runtime (2363
  ms vs 5972 ms at batch 16×256). Numerical validation against ORT on the same
  graph: sentence_embedding max diff 7e-6 with optimization on, so the Einsum/If
  risks and issue #1381 never materialized.

  The int8 export is rejected. It loads, is 4× smaller and 1.5× faster — but its
  "≥0.96 cosine" claim is false. Measured against fp32: mean cosine 0.909, and
  14% of top-1 nearest-neighbour lookups return a different result. Not worth
  it.

  Measured: ~6–7 seq/s at batch 32, 82–127 ms single-query latency, ~1.1 GB peak
  RSS. Consequence for the design: a full 40k-chunk index is ~1h45m, so the
  code index must be a background resumable job, never a startup step.

  Storage: validated against a real database, not docs

  Every load-bearing claim now has a passing test behind it. Three things the
  probe corrected:

  - _-prefixed relation names are reserved for transaction-scoped ephemerals —
  :create _meta fails with "read-only transaction". Renamed to meta.
  - Aggregations go in the rule head; m = max(x) in the body is
  eval::no_implementation.
  - export_relations_str wraps output as {"ok":…,"data":…} but the importer
  wants the bare map — export/import didn't compose until I unwrapped it.

  And one genuine design bug the tests caught: superseding a fact removed it
  from HNSW recall but not from the BM25 leg, so it kept coming back through the
  other half of the hybrid query. Fixed with extract_filter: live on the FTS
  index. Notably that same fix empties the LSH index, so dedupe enforces
  liveness with a join instead — documented in schema.rs so nobody "fixes" it
  back.

  Also: mnestic-rocks 0.1.10 doesn't build on GCC 16 (vendored RocksDB headers
  omit <cstdint>). Workaround is CXXFLAGS="-include cstdint" — and importantly
  not CFLAGS, which breaks zstd-sys.

  State

  10/10 tests pass (cargo test -p artist-memory).

  One implementation note worth knowing: rten_text::Tokenizer is neither Send
  nor Sync, so it can't be shared across the blocking pool — each blocking
  thread builds its own from a shared JSON source via a thread-local.

      418 +    /// Ids of every fact currently stored, live or retired.  
      419 +    pub async fn all_fact_ids(&self) -> Result<Vec<i64>> {    
      420 +        let rows = self                                       
      421 +            .script("?[fact_id] := *fact{fact_id}", BTreeMap::
          +new(), false)                                                 
      422 +            .await?;                                          
      423 +        Ok(rows.rows.iter().filter_map(|r| r[0].get_int()).col
          +lect())                                                       
      424 +    }                                                         
      425 +                                                              
      426 +    /// Reconcile the store against the session log.          
      427 +    ///                                                       
      428 +    /// Replay runs over `visible_events`, so a rewind that ma
          +sks a                                                         
      429 +    /// `memory.written` event makes the corresponding fact di
          +sappear here too —                                            
      430 +    /// the same property `TodoStore` gets, for the same reaso
          +n. Facts present                                              
      431 +    /// in the store but absent from the visible log are dropp
          +ed; facts in the                                              
      432 +    /// log but missing from the store are reported so the cal
          +ler can re-embed                                              
      433 +    /// and reinsert them (embedding is not recorded in the ev
          +ent, since it is                                              
      434 +    /// derivable and would bloat the log by 3 KB per fact).  
      435 +    pub async fn reconcile(&self, events: &[artist_session::En
          +velope]) -> Result<Reconciliation> {                          
      436 +        let mut expected: std::collections::BTreeMap<i64, arti
          +st_session::MemoryWritten> =                                  
      437 +            Default::default();                               
      438 +        let mut retired: std::collections::BTreeSet<i64> = Def
          +ault::default();                                              
      439 +                                                              
      440 +        for envelope in artist_session::visible_events(events)
          + {                                                            
      441 +            if let artist_session::SessionEvent::MemoryWritten
          +(written) = envelope.event() {                                
      442 +                if written.scope != self.scope.as_str() {     
      443 +                    continue;                                 
      444 +                }                                             
      445 +                if let Some(old) = written.superseded {       
      446 +                    retired.insert(old);                      
      447 +                }                                             
      448 +                expected.insert(written.fact_id, written);    
      449 +            }                                                 
      450 +        }                                                     
      451 +                                                              
      452 +        let mut removed = Vec::new();                         
      453 +        for id in self.all_fact_ids().await? {                
      454 +            if !expected.contains_key(&id) {                  
      455 +                self.forget(id).await?;                       
      456 +                removed.push(id);                             
      457 +            }                                                 
      458 +        }                                                     
      459 +                                                              
      460 +        let present: std::collections::BTreeSet<i64> =        
      461 +            self.all_fact_ids().await?.into_iter().collect(); 
      462 +        let missing = expected                                
      463 +            .into_values()                                    
      464 +            .filter(|w| !present.contains(&w.fact_id))        
      465 +            .collect();                                       
      466 +                                                              
      467 +        // Re-apply retirements: a rewind can restore a fact t
          +hat a later                                                   
      468 +        // supersession had retired, and vice versa.          
      469 +        for old in retired {                                  
      470 +            if present.contains(&old) {                       
      471 +                self.mark_retired(old).await?;                
      472 +            }                                                 
      473 +        }                                                     
      474 +                                                              
      475 +        Ok(Reconciliation { removed, missing })               
      476 +    }                                                         
      477 +                                                              
      478 +    async fn mark_retired(&self, id: i64) -> Result<()> {     
      479 +        let mut params = BTreeMap::new();                     
      480 +        params.insert("id".to_string(), DataValue::from(id)); 
      481 +        self.script(                                          
      482 +            "?[fact_id, live] := fact_id = $id, live = false :
          +update fact {fact_id => live}",                               
      483 +            params,                                           
      484 +            true,

      491 +    pub async fn put_facts_with_ids(&self, facts: &[(i64, NewF
          +act)]) -> Result<()> {                                        
      492 +        if facts.is_empty() {

      494 +        }

      563 +    /// resumable background job rather than a startup stall. 
      564 +    pub async fn chunk_hashes(&self, path: &str) -> Result<Vec
          +<(i64, String)>> {

          +));

          +c<f32>)]) -> Result<Vec<i64>> {                               
      584 +        if chunks.is_empty() {                                
      585 +            return Ok(Vec::new());

      626 +    pub async fn remove_chunks(&self, ids: &[i64]) -> Result<(
          +)> {                                                          
      627 +        if ids.is_empty() {                                   
      628 +            return Ok(());                                    
      629 +        }                                                     
      630 +        let mut params = BTreeMap::new();                     
      631 +        params.insert(                                        
      632 +            "ids".to_string(),                                
      633 +            DataValue::List(ids.iter().map(|i| DataValue::List
          +(vec![DataValue::from(*i)])).collect()),                      
      634 +        );

      636 +            "?[chunk_id] <- $ids :rm chunk {chunk_id}",       
      637 +            params,

      641 +        Ok(())

          +{

      655 +    pub async fn chunk_count(&self) -> Result<usize> {        
      656 +        let rows = self                                       
      657 +            .script("?[count(chunk_id)] := *chunk{chunk_id}", 
          +BTreeMap::new(), false)                                       
      658 +            .await?;                                          
      659 +        Ok(rows                                               
      660 +            .rows                                             
      661 +            .first()                                          
      662 +            .and_then(|r| r.first())                          
      663 +            .and_then(|v| v.get_int())                        
      664 +            .unwrap_or(0) as usize)                           
      665 +    }

      667 +    /// Hybrid search over indexed source.                    
      668 +    pub async fn search_chunks(                               
      669 +        &self,                                                
      670 +        query_text: &str,                                     
      671 +        query_vec: &[f32],                                    
      672 +        k: usize,                                             
      673 +    ) -> Result<Vec<CodeHit>> {                               
      674 +        let leg_k = (k * 4).max(20);                          
      675 +        let ef = (leg_k * 4).max(100);                        
      676 +        let mut params = BTreeMap::new();                     
      677 +        params.insert(                                        
      678 +            "qv".to_string(),                                 
      679 +            DataValue::List(query_vec.iter().map(|f| DataValue
          +::from(*f as f64)).collect()),                                
      680 +        );                                                    
      681 +        params.insert("qt".to_string(), DataValue::from(query_
          +text));                                                       
      682 +        let script = format!(                                 
      683 +            r#"                                               
      684 +            sem[id, score] := ~chunk:emb_idx{{ chunk_id: id | 
          +query: vec($qv), k: {leg_k},                                  
      685 +                                               ef: {ef}, bind_
          +distance: __d }}, score = -__d                                
      686 +            txt[id, score] := ~chunk:body_fts{{ chunk_id: id |
          + query: $qt, k: {leg_k},                                      
      687 +                                                bind_score: sc
          +ore }}                                                        
      688 +            combined[__lid, id, score] := sem[id, score], __li
          +d = 'semantic'                                                
      689 +            combined[__lid, id, score] := txt[id, score], __li
          +d = 'text'                                                    
      690 +            fused[id, score] <~ ReciprocalRankFusion(combined[
          +__lid, id, score], k: 60.0)                                   
      691 +            ?[id, score, path, lang, start_line, end_line, bod
          +y] :=                                                         
      692 +                fused[id, score],                             
      693 +                *chunk{{chunk_id: id, path, lang, start_line, 
          +end_line, body}}                                              
      694 +            :order -score                                     
      695 +            :limit {k}                                        
      696 +            "#                                                
      697 +        );                                                    
      698 +        let rows = self.script_owned(script, params, false).aw
          +ait?;                                                         
      699 +        Ok(rows                                               
      700 +            .rows                                             
      701 +            .iter()                                           
      702 +            .filter_map(|r| {                                 
      703 +                Some(CodeHit {                                
      704 +                    score: r[1].get_float()?,                 
      705 +                    path: r[2].get_str()?.to_owned(),         
      706 +                    lang: r[3].get_str().unwrap_or_default().t
          +o_owned(),                                                    
      707 +                    start_line: r[4].get_int()? as u32,       
      708 +                    end_line: r[5].get_int()? as u32,         
      709 +                    body: r[6].get_str()?.to_owned(),         
      710 +                })                                            
      711 +            })                                                
      712 +            .collect())                                       
      713 +    }

● The dedupe test is genuinely flaky — 2 of 3 runs fail. MinHash-LSH is
  probabilistic, and my test pair sits right on the 0.8 Jaccard threshold, so
  it's a coin flip. The threshold itself is correct (strict is right here: a
  false positive suppresses a genuinely new fact, which is worse than storing a
  redundant one). The test was wrong to probe the decision boundary.
