      394 +// -----------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      395 +// Write trigger 2 + read channel 3: the agent hook           
      396 +// -----------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      397 +                                                              
      398 +/// Phrases that mark the model committing to a decision with 
          +a reason.                                                     
      399 +///                                                           
      400 +/// Deliberately narrow: this fires mid-stream on every turn, 
          +so a loose pattern                                            
      401 +/// would bury the store in restatements of things the transcr
          +ipt already says.                                             
      402 +const DECISION_PATTERNS: &[&str] = &[                         
      403 +    r"(?i)\b(I'?ll|I\s+will|going\s+to)\s+use\s+\w+\s+(because
          +|since|so\s+that)\b",                                         
      404 +    r"(?i)\b(chose|choosing|picked|opting\s+for)\s+\w+\s+(over
          +|instead\s+of)\s+\w+\b",                                      
      405 +    r"(?i)\bthe\s+reason\s+.{0,60}\bis\s+that\b",             
      406 +    r"(?i)\bwon'?t\s+work\s+because\b",                       
      407 +];                                                            
      408 +                                                              
      409 +/// Shared state between the streaming hook and the completion
          +-call injector.                                               
      410 +struct HookState {                                            
      411 +    /// Tail of the current model turn, bounded like the rules
          + matcher's window.                                            
      412 +    window: String,                                           
      413 +    /// Recalled facts waiting to be injected on the next comp
          +letion call.                                                  
      414 +    pending: Vec<artist_memory::Hit>,                         
      415 +    /// Recall is fired at most once per turn; the model resta
          +ting itself should                                            
      416 +    /// not cause a second lookup.                            
      417 +    fired_this_turn: bool,                                    
      418 +}                                                             
      419 +                                                              
      420 +/// Matching window in bytes, mirroring the stream rules defau
          +lt.                                                           
      421 +const WINDOW_BYTES: usize = 4096;                             
      422 +/// Evaluate only once this many new bytes accumulate, as the 
          +rules matcher does.                                           
      423 +const COALESCE_BYTES: usize = 64;                             
      424 +                                                              
      425 +/// Observes the model's own output and turns it into memory r
          +eads and writes.                                              
      426 +///                                                           
      427 +/// This is the stream-rules mechanism pointed at a different 
          +purpose, with one                                             
      428 +/// critical difference: it **never aborts**. Stream rules ter
          +minate the run to                                             
      429 +/// keep a mistake out of context; memory only ever adds conte
          +xt, so a match                                                
      430 +/// here schedules work and returns `keep`. The retrieval it k
          +icks off runs in                                              
      431 +/// the background and is collected by `on_completion_call`, w
          +hich is why it                                                
      432 +/// never blocks the stream — the model is still generating wh
          +ile it runs.                                                  
      433 +#[derive(Clone)]                                              
      434 +pub(crate) struct MemoryHook {                                
      435 +    handle: MemoryHandle,                                     
      436 +    decisions: Arc<RegexSet>,                                 
      437 +    state: Arc<std::sync::Mutex<HookState>>,                  
      438 +    /// Facts recalled in the background, ready for the next c
          +ompletion call.                                               
      439 +    inbox: Arc<std::sync::Mutex<Vec<artist_memory::Hit>>>,    
      440 +    auto_write: bool,                                         
      441 +}                                                             
      442 +                                                              
      443 +impl MemoryHook {                                             
      444 +    pub fn new(handle: MemoryHandle, auto_write: bool) -> Self
          + {                                                            
      445 +        Self {                                                
      446 +            handle,                                           
      447 +            decisions: Arc::new(                              
      448 +                RegexSet::new(DECISION_PATTERNS).unwrap_or_els
          +e(|_| RegexSet::empty()),                                     
      449 +            ),                                                
      450 +            state: Arc::new(std::sync::Mutex::new(HookState { 
      451 +                window: String::new(),                        
      452 +                pending: Vec::new(),                          
      453 +                fired_this_turn: false,                       
      454 +            })),                                              
      455 +            inbox: Arc::new(std::sync::Mutex::new(Vec::new()))
          +,                                                             
      456 +            auto_write,                                       
      457 +        }                                                     
      458 +    }                                                         
      459 +                                                              
      460 +    fn lock(&self) -> std::sync::MutexGuard<'_, HookState> {  
      461 +        self.state.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner())  
      462 +    }                                                         
      463 +                                                              
      464 +    /// Feed streamed text; returns an excerpt when recall sho
          +uld be triggered.                                             
      465 +    fn observe(&self, delta: &str) -> Option<String> {        
      466 +        let mut state = self.lock();                          
      467 +        if state.fired_this_turn {                            
      468 +            return None;                                      
      469 +        }                                                     
      470 +        state.window.push_str(delta);                         
      471 +        if state.window.len() > WINDOW_BYTES {                
      472 +            let cut = state.window.len() - WINDOW_BYTES;      
      473 +            let cut = floor_char_boundary(&state.window, cut);
      474 +            state.window.drain(..cut);                        
      475 +        }                                                     
      476 +        if delta.len() < COALESCE_BYTES && !delta.contains('\n
          +') {                                                          
      477 +            return None;                                      
      478 +        }                                                     
      479 +        if !self.decisions.is_match(&state.window) {          
      480 +            return None;                                      
      481 +        }                                                     
      482 +        state.fired_this_turn = true;                         
      483 +        Some(state.window.clone())                            
      484 +    }                                                         
      485 +                                                              
      486 +    /// Kick off a recall without blocking the stream.        
      487 +    fn schedule_recall(&self, excerpt: String) {              
      488 +        let handle = self.handle.clone();                     
      489 +        let inbox = Arc::clone(&self.inbox);                  
      490 +        tokio::spawn(async move {                             
      491 +            let hits = handle.recall(&excerpt).await;         
      492 +            if hits.is_empty() {                              
      493 +                return;                                       
      494 +            }                                                 
      495 +            let mut slot = inbox.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.i
          +nto_inner());                                                 
      496 +            slot.extend(hits);                                
      497 +        });                                                   
      498 +    }                                                         
      499 +                                                              
      500 +    /// Extract and store a fact from a completed commit, in t
          +he background.                                                
      501 +    fn schedule_commit_write(&self, subject: String) {        
      502 +        if !self.auto_write {                                 
      503 +            return;                                           
      504 +        }                                                     
      505 +        let handle = self.handle.clone();                     
      506 +        tokio::spawn(async move {                             
      507 +            let text = format!("Committed: {subject}");       
      508 +            let _ = handle                                    
      509 +                .remember(Scope::Project, "", "", &text, &text
          +, "commit")                                                   
      510 +                .await;                                       
      511 +        });                                                   
      512 +    }                                                         
      513 +                                                              
      514 +    /// Drain whatever recall has landed, deduplicated by fact
          + id.                                                          
      515 +    fn take_recalled(&self) -> Vec<artist_memory::Hit> {      
      516 +        let mut inbox = self.inbox.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e
          +.into_inner());                                               
      517 +        let mut hits: Vec<_> = inbox.drain(..).collect();     
      518 +        let mut state = self.lock();                          
      519 +        hits.append(&mut state.pending);                      
      520 +        let mut seen = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();     
      521 +        hits.retain(|h| seen.insert((h.scope.as_str(), h.id)))
          +;                                                             
      522 +        hits                                                  
      523 +    }                                                         
      524 +                                                              
      525 +    /// Called at the start of each user turn.                
      526 +    pub fn reset_turn(&self) {                                
      527 +        let mut state = self.lock();                          
      528 +        state.window.clear();                                 
      529 +        state.fired_this_turn = false;                        
      530 +    }                                                         
      531 +}                                                             
      532 +                                                              
      533 +fn floor_char_boundary(value: &str, mut index: usize) -> usize
          + {                                                            
      534 +    while index < value.len() && !value.is_char_boundary(index
          +) {                                                           
      535 +        index += 1;                                           
      536 +    }                                                         
      537 +    index                                                     
      538 +}                                                             
      539 +

      554 +impl AgentHook for MemoryHook {                               
      555 +    fn observes(&self, kind: StepEventKind) -> bool {         
      556 +        // Inert when memory is off, so a disabled subsystem c
          +osts nothing on                                               
      557 +        // the streaming path.                                
      558 +        self.enabled()                                        
      559 +            && matches!(                                      
      560 +                kind,                                         
      561 +                StepEventKind::TextDelta                      
      562 +                    | StepEventKind::CompletionCall           
      563 +                    | StepEventKind::ToolResult               
      564 +            )                                                 
      565 +    }                                                         
      566 +                                                              
      567 +    /// Read channel 3. Injects outside ordinary history, so r
          +ecalled facts                                                 
      568 +    /// survive compaction and handoff by construction — the s
          +ame property the                                              
      569 +    /// session-persistent stream rules rely on.              
      570 +    async fn on_completion_call(                              
      571 +        &self,                                                
      572 +        _context: &HookContext,                               
      573 +        _event: CompletionCallEvent<'_>,                      
      574 +    ) -> CompletionCallAction {                               
      575 +        let hits = self.take_recalled();                      
      576 +        if hits.is_empty() {                                  
      577 +            return CompletionCallAction::continue_run();      
      578 +        }                                                     
      579 +        let text = artist_memory::render(&hits);              
      580 +        if text.is_empty() {                                  
      581 +            return CompletionCallAction::continue_run();      
      582 +        }                                                     
      583 +        CompletionCallAction::patch(RequestPatch::new().extra_
          +context([Document {                                           
      584 +            id: "artist-memory".to_owned(),                   
      585 +            text,                                             
      586 +            additional_props: Default::default(),             
      587 +        }]))                                                  
      588 +    }                                                         
      589 +                                                              
      590 +    /// Write trigger 2. Never aborts: stream rules terminate 
          +a run to keep a                                               
      591 +    /// mistake out of context, but memory only ever adds cont
          +ext, so a match                                               
      592 +    /// here schedules work and lets the turn continue untouch
          +ed.                                                           
      593 +    async fn on_text_delta(                                   
      594 +        &self,                                                
      595 +        _context: &HookContext,                               
      596 +        event: TextDelta<'_>,                                 
      597 +    ) -> ObservationAction {                                  
      598 +        if let Some(excerpt) = self.observe(event.delta) {    
      599 +            self.schedule_recall(excerpt);                    
      600 +        }                                                     
      601 +        ObservationAction::continue_run()                     
      602 +    }                                                         
      603 +                                                              
      604 +    /// Write trigger 3. A commit is a completed unit of work 
          +— low frequency                                               
      605 +    /// and coherent, unlike a turn boundary.                 
      606 +    async fn on_tool_result(                                  
      607 +        &self,                                                
      608 +        _context: &HookContext,                               
      609 +        event: ToolResultEvent<'_>,                           
      610 +    ) -> ToolResultAction {                                   
      611 +        if self.enabled() {                                   
      612 +            let rendered = event.presentation.render();       
      613 +            if let Some(subject) = commit_summary(&event.argum
          +ents.to_string(), &rendered) {                                
      614 +                self.schedule_commit_write(subject);          
      615 +            }                                                 
      616 +        }                                                     
      617 +        ToolResultAction::keep()                              
      618 +    }                                                         
      619 +}                                                             
      620 +

❯ Diagnosis from wV:p4 on the mnestic-rocks build failure that is now blocking  
  artist-agent (and therefore everyone downstream): it is the well-known GCC    
  13+ breakage. The vendored RocksDB in mnestic-rocks 0.1.10 relies on          
  <cstdint> being transitively included, which GCC 13 stopped doing, so you get 
  a wall of "uint64_t has not been declared" in                                 
  rocksdb/db/blob/blob_file_meta.h. Nothing to do with your Rust code.          
  Workaround that does not require patching the crate: build with               
  CXXFLAGS="-include cstdint" (I am using env CXXFLAGS="-include cstdint" cargo 
  test locally). For a durable fix, set that in .cargo/config.toml env, or      
  bump mnestic-rocks to a version whose vendored rocksdb has the includes, or   
  switch the memory store off rocksdb. Flagging because as long as artist-agent 
  depends on artist-memory, a cold build of artist-agent fails for every agent  
  in this repo.                                                                 

● wV:p4 is right, and this is a cost I introduced: artist-memory now makes every
  agent's cold build fail unless they remember CXXFLAGS. The durable fix
  belongs in the repo, not in each person's shell.

  Listed 1 directory (ctrl+o to expand)

      778 +/// Open the memory subsystem, or return `None` when it is off
          + or unusable.                                                 
      779 +///                                                           
      780 +/// Every failure here degrades to "no memory" rather than abo
          +rting the session:                                            
      781 +/// a missing embedding model or an unreadable database should
          + cost recall, not                                             
      782 +/// the ability to work. The reason it is disabled by default 
          +is the same one —                                             
      783 +/// silently degrading to lexical-only recall would read as me
          +mory being broken.                                            
      784 +async fn open_memory(                                         
      785 +    config_root: &std::path::Path,                            
      786 +    project: &std::path::Path,                                
      787 +    settings: &settings::MemoryConfig,                        
      788 +    recorder: artist_session::Recorder,                       
      789 +    session_id: &str,                                         
      790 +) -> Option<artist_agent::memory::MemoryHandle> {             
      791 +    if !settings.enabled {                                    
      792 +        return None;                                          
      793 +    }                                                         
      794 +    let state_dir = project_state_dir(config_root, project).ok
          +()?;                                                          
      795 +    let memory = match artist_memory::Memory::open(config_root
          +, &state_dir).await {                                         
      796 +        Ok(memory) => memory,                                 
      797 +        Err(error) => {                                       
      798 +            eprintln!("warning: memory disabled, could not ope
          +n the store: {error}");                                       
      799 +            return None;                                      
      800 +        }                                                     
      801 +    };                                                        
      802 +    let model_dir = settings                                  
      803 +        .model_dir                                            
      804 +        .as_ref()                                             
      805 +        .map(|dir| {                                          
      806 +            let path = std::path::Path::new(dir);             
      807 +            if path.is_absolute() {                           
      808 +                path.to_path_buf()                            
      809 +            } else {                                          
      810 +                config_root.join(path)                        
      811 +            }                                                 
      812 +        })                                                    
      813 +        .unwrap_or_else(|| config_root.join("models").join("co
          +de-embed"));                                                  
      814 +    let embedder = match artist_memory::Embedder::load(&model_
          +dir, settings.dim).await {                                    
      815 +        Ok(embedder) => Some(embedder),                       
      816 +        Err(error) => {                                       
      817 +            eprintln!(                                        
      818 +                "warning: memory recall will be lexical only, 
          +no embedding model at {}: {error}",                           
      819 +                model_dir.display()                           
      820 +            );                                                
      821 +            None                                              
      822 +        }                                                     
      823 +    };                                                        
      824 +    Some(artist_agent::memory::MemoryHandle::new(             
      825 +        memory,                                               
      826 +        embedder,                                             
      827 +        recorder,                                             
      828 +        session_id.to_owned(),                                
      829 +    ))                                                        
      830 +}

● Update(docs/architecture.md)
  ⎿  Added 82 lines
      297  
      298  ---
      299  
      300 +## Durable memory                                             
      301 +                                                              
      302 +Sessions are event-sourced but nothing read them back, so ever
          +y session started                                             
      303 +cold. `artist-memory` gives the harness two corpora that outli
          +ve a session:                                                 
      304 +**facts** (curated preferences, constraints, decisions and the
          +ir rationale) and                                             
      305 +an optional **code index** (AST-chunked, embedded source).    
      306 +                                                              
      307 +This generalizes the invariant the todo list already establish
          +ed — *harness-owned                                           
      308 +state survives a context wipe without being summarized into lo
          +ssy prose*                                                    
      309 +(`docs/profiles.md`, invariant 7). Memory is never part of the
          + conversation: it                                             
      310 +lives in its own store, is recorded as `memory.written` events
          +, and is injected                                             
      311 +only when relevant.                                           
      312 +                                                              
      313 +**Storage.** One [Mnestic](https://github.com/shuruheel/mnesti
          +c) database per                                               
      314 +scope — a global one under `<config>/memory/` for preferences 
          +that follow you                                               
      315 +between checkouts, and a project one beside the existing tool 
          +state at                                                      
      316 +`<config>/tools/<project-hash>/memory.rocks`. They are separat
          +e databases rather                                            
      317 +than one relation with a scope column because a query-time HNS
          +W `filter:` is                                                
      318 +only a post-filter over the `ef` candidate pool, so partitioni
          +ng by filter would                                            
      319 +silently drop results.                                        
      320 +                                                              
      321 +Relations: `fact` (with a `<F32; 768>` embedding, `live`, `sup
          +erseded_by`),                                                 
      322 +`fact_history` (a `TxTime` audit sibling), `rel`, and `chunk`.
          + Retrieval fuses                                              
      323 +an HNSW leg with a BM25 leg through Mnestic's built-in `Recipr
          +ocalRankFusion`.                                              
      324 +                                                              
      325 +Three constraints are load-bearing and were verified against a
          + live database                                                
      326 +rather than read from documentation:                          
      327 +                                                              
      328 +- A `TxTime` relation **rejects every index and trigger**, and
          + search atoms take                                            
      329 +  no validity clause — so bitemporality and search cannot coex
          +ist on one                                                    
      330 +  relation. Searchable facts therefore carry `live`/`supersede
          +d_by`, and                                                    
      331 +  `fact_history` is an audit trail only.                      
      332 +- HNSW `filter:`/`radius:` are **post-filters over the `ef` po
          +ol**, contradicting                                           
      333 +  the upstream docs. The `live` condition is built into the in
          +dex at creation                                               
      334 +  time so superseded facts leave recall automatically on `:upd
          +ate`.                                                         
      335 +- Every *searchable* index over `fact` needs that condition, n
          +ot just the vector                                            
      336 +  one: an unfiltered BM25 index resurrects superseded facts th
          +rough the other                                               
      337 +  half of the fused query. `::fts` spells it `extract_filter`.
          + The `::lsh`                                                  
      338 +  dedupe index is the exception — an `extract_filter` there em
          +pties it, so                                                  
      339 +  liveness is enforced by a join in the query instead.        
      340 +                                                              
      341 +**The store is a projection, not the record.** Mnestic's Rocks
          +DB backend commits                                            
      342 +without syncing its WAL and the durability opt-in does not exi
          +st in the pinned                                              
      343 +release, so facts are recorded as `memory.written` session eve
          +nts and                                                       
      344 +reconciled on open. That is also what makes memory **rewind-aw
          +are for free**:                                               
      345 +replay runs over `visible_events`, so rewinding past a write r
          +emoves the fact,                                              
      346 +exactly as with `todo.updated`.                               
      347 +                                                              
      348 +**Embeddings are local.** `rten` (pure-Rust ONNX, no C++ runti
          +me) runs                                                      
      349 +CodeRankEmbed on CPU. Measured on a 13th-gen i7: ~6–7 sequence
          +s/sec at batch 32,                                            
      350 +82–127 ms for a single query, numerically identical to ONNX Ru
          +ntime to ~1e-6.                                               
      351 +No code leaves the machine and no API key is involved. The pub
          +lished int8 export                                            
      352 +is deliberately **not** used — it measured 0.909 mean cosine a
          +gainst fp32 and                                               
      353 +moved 14% of top-1 results.                                   
      354 +                                                              
      355 +**Writes.** A `memory` tool for deliberate recording, plus thr
          +ee automatic                                                  
      356 +triggers chosen because they carry signal rather than because 
          +they are                                                      
      357 +convenient: a user correction (a preference being stated out l
          +oud), a model                                                 
      358 +decision stated mid-stream with its rationale, and a successfu
          +l `git commit`.                                               
      359 +All three are fire-and-forget; none may delay a stream or the 
          +input box.                                                    
      360 +Compaction is deliberately *not* a trigger.                   
      361 +                                                              
      362 +**Reads.** Three channels, all pre-existing mechanisms: the `m
          +emory` tool                                                   
      363 +(model-pull), a prompt-conditioned prepend riding the user tur
          +n beside skill                                                
      364 +sections (never the preamble, which must stay a stable prompt-
          +cache prefix), and                                            
      365 +`RequestPatch::extra_context` injection from a hook — the only
          + channel that                                                 
      366 +survives compaction and handoff by construction. The hook shar
          +es the stream                                                 
      367 +rules' matcher shape but **never aborts**: rules terminate a r
          +un to keep a                                                  
      368 +mistake out of context, whereas memory only ever adds context.
      369 +                                                              
      370 +Configured under `[memory]` in `settings.toml`, off by default
          + because the                                                  
      371 +subsystem needs a local embedding model and silently degrading
          + to lexical-only                                              
      372 +recall would read as memory simply not working.               
      373 +                                                              
      374 +> **Build note.** `mnestic-rocks 0.1.10` vendors a RocksDB who
          +se headers rely on                                            
      375 +> `<cstdint>` arriving transitively, which GCC 13+ stopped doi
          +ng. The workspace                                             
      376 +> `.cargo/config.toml` sets `CXXFLAGS = "-include cstdint"` so
          + cold builds work                                             
      377 +> without per-developer setup. It must not also be set for `CF
          +LAGS` — that                                                  
      378 +> breaks `zstd-sys`.                                          
      379 +                                                              
      380 +---                                                           
      381 +                                                              
      382  ## Tools and workspace
      383  
      384  | Tool | Role |

      367 +const MAX_CORRECTION_CHARS: usize = 400;                      
      368 +                                                              
      369 +/// Store a user correction, in the background, if the text lo
          +oks like one.                                                 
      370 +///                                                           
      371 +/// The user's own words go in unmodified. Distilling them thr
          +ough the model                                                
      372 +/// would cost a call and lose the phrasing that made the pref
          +erence clear —                                                
      373 +/// and the user already said exactly what they meant.        
      374 +pub(crate) fn capture_correction(writer: MemoryWriter, text: &
          +str) {                                                        
      375 +    let text = text.trim();                                   
      376 +    if text.is_empty() || text.chars().count() > MAX_CORRECTIO
          +N_CHARS {

      378 +    }

      380 +        return;

      385 +            .remember(Scope::Project, "", "", &text, &text, "c
          +orrection")                                                   
      386 +            .await;                                           
      387 +    });

● The memory system is built and verified. Here's where it landed.

  What shipped

  A new artist-memory crate plus artist-agent/src/memory.rs, fully wired into
  the CLI and TUI:

  - Storage — two Mnestic/RocksDB stores (global + project), with facts, a
  bitemporal audit sibling, and a code-chunk relation. Retrieval fuses HNSW and
  BM25 through Mnestic's built-in ReciprocalRankFusion.
  - Embeddings — rten + CodeRankEmbed, entirely local. No egress, no API key.
  - Writes — a memory tool, plus three automatic triggers: user corrections,
  model decisions stated mid-stream, and successful commits. Compaction is
  deliberately not one.
  - Reads — model-pull tool, prompt-conditioned prepend on the user turn, and
  extra_context injection that survives compaction and handoff.
  - Ops — artist memory list|search|export|import|reindex|verify, [memory] in
  settings.toml, docs in architecture.md.

  Tests: 17 passing in artist-memory (plus 3 model-gated, run and passing
  against the real 548 MB model), 125/125 in artist-agent, 60 in artist-session.

  The two results that changed the design

  The spike verdict was GO, but not as expected. rten turned out to be ~2.5× 
  faster than ONNX Runtime on this model, and numerically identical to 7e-6 with
  optimization on — so none of the flagged risks (Einsum, If subgraphs, the
  #1381 optimizer miscompile) materialized. But the int8 export is unusable: its
  card claims ≥0.96 cosine; measured against fp32 it's 0.909, and it moves 14% 
  of top-1 lookups. Rejected in favour of fp32.

  Mnestic's headline feature can't do what it was picked for. A TxTime relation
  rejects every index and trigger, and search atoms take no validity clause — so
  bitemporality and search are mutually exclusive on one relation. I flagged
  this before you confirmed the substrate; it's now empirically verified
  (eval::txtime_unsupported_op) and worked around with live/superseded_by plus a
  create-time index filter.

  A test caught the subtle version of that: superseding removed a fact from
  vector recall but the BM25 leg kept resurrecting it through the other half of
  the fused query. Fixed with extract_filter on the FTS index — which, in turn,
  empties the LSH index if applied there, so dedupe enforces liveness by join
  instead. All three behaviours are documented in schema.rs so nobody "fixes"
  them back.

  Collisions, resolved

  Three sibling agents were editing this repo concurrently. Per your instruction
  I coordinated through herdr rather than working around them: I flagged their
  artist-canvas reqwest break, their missing SessionEvent variants (the
  identical trap I'd hit an hour earlier), and their stale-build misattribution
  of a test failure to me; they flagged the RocksDB/GCC issue to me. I didn't
  edit any of their files.

  One thing I'd surface: my crate made every cold build in the workspace fail,
  because mnestic-rocks needs CXXFLAGS="-include cstdint" on GCC 13+. I put that
  in .cargo/config.toml rather than leaving four agents to each rediscover it —
  and not in CFLAGS, which breaks zstd-sys.

  Not done

  The code index is wired but unproven at scale. index_code defaults to false
  and no full pass has run — that's ~1h45m for 40k chunks at measured
  throughput. It also refreshes at session start rather than reacting to the
  existing fff-search watcher, which is the obvious next step. And the rel
  relation exists but nothing writes fact-to-fact edges yet, so the graph
  capability that partly justified Mnestic is available but unused.

  Two things outside my scope are currently red: artist-cli's /canvas
  slash-command test and the new artist-computer crate, both sibling
  work-in-progress.

✻ Cooked for 1h 10m 5s

❯ Explain what this means simply and practically as if im an idiot "A TxTime    
  relation rejects every index and trigger, and search atoms take no validity   
  clause — so bitemporality and search are mutually exclusive on one relation." 

● Plain version: the table type that remembers history is the one table type 
  you're not allowed to make searchable.

  Breaking the sentence into its three pieces:

  "TxTime relation" — a special kind of table where the database stamps every
  write with the time it happened and keeps every old version forever. That's
  the time-travel table. You can ask it "what did this row look like last
  Tuesday?"

  "rejects every index" — an index is the thing that makes search fast. There's
  one for semantic search (vectors) and one for keyword search (BM25). Mnestic
  flat-out refuses to let you put either on a time-travel table. Try it and you
  get an error.

  "search atoms take no validity clause" — even setting that aside, the query
  syntax has no way to write "search as of last Tuesday." The feature isn't just
  blocked, it's not expressible.

  The analogy

  It's a filing cabinet that keeps every draft of every document forever, neatly
  dated. Great. But the rules say you may not attach a card catalogue to that
  cabinet. So you can leaf through its history by hand, but you can't look
  anything up.

  Why it mattered here

  Time travel was a big part of why Mnestic looked right. Memory has a "facts
  get corrected" problem — you tell me you prefer X, later you switch to Y, and
  I need to stop recalling X. Time travel handles that beautifully in theory:
  never delete anything, just ask "what's true now."

  Except memory's entire job is search. If the fact table can't be searched,
  it's not a memory system, it's a diary. So the elegant feature and the
  required feature can't be on the same table.

  What I did instead

  Ordinary searchable table, plus a live column that's just true/false.
  Correcting a fact flips the old one to live = false, and the search indexes
  are built to skip non-live rows — so it drops out of recall automatically. The
  time-travel table still exists alongside, but purely as an audit log nothing
  queries in normal operation.

  It works, and the tests prove it. But be clear-eyed about the trade: we ended 
  up with a boolean flag where the pitch was a time machine — and a boolean flag
  is what any database would have handed us. That's why I flagged it to you
  before you locked in the substrate: it removed most of Mnestic's specific
  advantage over the simpler options. You chose to keep it anyway, which is
  defensible — you still get Datalog, graph queries, and real BM25 in one engine
  — but the differentiator that made it obvious isn't there.

✻ Churned for 27s

❯ Why did mnestic make this choice? WTF was blocking the divine form?           

● The error message actually tells you, and it's more interesting than "they
  didn't get around to it."

  The literal reason

  The rejection text is: "statement-time index/trigger maintenance is 
  incompatible with buffered commit-time stamping."

  Unpack that. A TxTime column is engine-assigned at commit — you're not allowed
  to supply it, that's the whole point of a transaction-time column (you can't
  forge when something happened). But an index is maintained per-row, as each 
  statement runs: when you :put a row, the HNSW insertion and the FTS postings
  are written right then.

  See the collision? At the moment index maintenance needs to run, the row's 
  primary key doesn't exist yet. The TxTime part gets filled in later, at
  commit. An index entry is fundamentally a pointer to a row by its key — and
  you can't write a pointer to a key that hasn't been decided.

  There's corroborating evidence in a quirk I found separately: on a TxTime
  relation, a script does not see its own writes. The stamping is buffered until
  commit. So it's not a lazy refusal — the whole table type is built around
  "the identity of this row is undetermined until the transaction ends."

  (That mechanism is my inference from the error text plus the observed 
  behaviour; the error text and the read-your-writes quirk are verified.)

  The deeper reason — what would a temporal vector index even be?

  Ask what you're requesting when you say "semantic search, as of last Tuesday."

  An HNSW index is a precomputed navigable graph. Its edges were built by
  comparing vectors that existed at build time. A temporal query is a filter.
  You cannot retroactively filter a precomputed graph without breaking the thing
  that makes it work: if you skip nodes during traversal, you can disconnect
  the graph and silently lose results that were only reachable through the
  skipped ones.

  So your options are all bad: rebuild the index per timepoint (absurd), or
  degrade to a scan (then why have an index).

  And here's the part that ties the room together

  The HNSW post-filter gotcha I hit earlier is the same problem wearing a 
  different hat. Remember filter: turning out to be a post-filter over the ef
  pool rather than a true in-search filter, silently returning fewer than k
  rows? Same root cause. Filtering a graph traversal is hard, so implementations
  either post-filter (and lose recall) or refuse outright.

  Filtered ANN is an active research area with real papers on it — it is not a
  thing Mnestic simply forgot.

  Fundamental, or just young?

  Honestly: a hard problem sitting under a soft implementation choice.

  The soft part — "buffered commit-time stamping" is an architecture decision,
  not a law. Assign timestamps optimistically at statement start, or defer index
  maintenance to commit, and it could work. Mnestic is two months old with one
  maintainer, and TxTime is their addition; upstream CozoDB doesn't have it at
  all.

  The proof it's implementable: upstream's Validity columns (valid-time, the
  other temporal axis) do permit an HNSW index. And the result is bad — the
  index keys include the validity column, so every historical version becomes 
  its own node in the graph. Your 5,000 facts, each corrected twice, become
  15,000 vectors, and a k-NN search hands you the same fact three times at three
  different ages, which you then filter out yourself.

  So Mnestic looked at what "allowed" actually gets you and refused instead.
  That's arguably the more honest call — a hard error beats an index that
  quietly returns stale duplicates.

  The bit that makes it sting less

  The divine form is mostly a fantasy requirement. Nobody asks "what did my 
  agent believe about my tab preferences last Tuesday?" They ask what it
  believes now — that's ~99% of queries.

  live: true/false is the degenerate case of temporal filtering where you only
  ever query the present. It's the right-sized tool, it's maintained inside the
  index for free via the create-time filter, and the time-travel table still
  sits alongside as an audit log for the rare forensic question. We lost the
  elegance, not the capability.

✻ Baked for 1m 3s

❯ What is actually the idiomatic fix that doesnt require any hacks or bullshit  
  though? Is it this, or is this a bolt on?                                     

● Direct answer: what's there is idiomatic — and it's idiomatic twice over. But
  there's one piece of it that is a bolt-on, and it's not the part you'd
  suspect.

  It has a name, and it predates vector search by 30 years

  live: bool + a partial index is Slowly Changing Dimension Type 2. The textbook
  SCD2 row is: surrogate key, business key, valid_from, valid_to, and
  is_current. That last flag is in every canonical implementation — Kimball's
  included — for exactly the reason we hit: almost all queries want "now," and
  you don't want each one dragging a temporal predicate through the planner.
  It's the standard denormalization of valid_to = infinity into something
  indexable.

  And the create-time filter: live is a partial index. In Postgres you'd write
  CREATE INDEX ... WHERE is_current. Nobody calls a partial index a hack; it's a
  first-class feature specifically for this shape.

  Nobody does as-of ANN. Not one system.

  I want to be concrete rather than hand-wavy:

  - Postgres + pgvector — is_current boolean, partial index. Identical shape to
  ours.
  - Elasticsearch/Lucene — immutable segments plus a deleted-docs bitmap. Update
  means delete-and-reindex. There is no "search as of last Tuesday," full stop.
  - Datomic / XTDB — the genuine divine form for the relational half: truly
  immutable, as-of and history on every query. They pull it off with persistent 
  (structurally-shared) index trees where every historical root is retained. And
  neither does vector search or BM25.

  So the divine form exists — for value/entity lookup. It has never been built
  for ANN, because a navigable-small-world graph is a precomputed artifact, not
  a queryable relation. Retaining every historical root of an HNSW graph means
  retaining every historical graph.

  The genuine bolt-on is fact_history

  Here's the part I'd criticize in my own work.

  I kept a fact_history TxTime sibling "as an audit trail." Look at what it
  actually is: a table that can carry no index, that nothing queries, that
  duplicates information the session event log already holds — and holds better,
  since memory.written carries origin, session id, and sequence number, while
  fact_history carries only text and a flag.

  It exists because the plan said "keep the audit trail," and I implemented the
  plan. But the event log is the audit trail. fact_history is write
  amplification for a feature with no reader. That's a vestigial organ, and it's
  the one thing here I'd call unearned.

  Two honest options: give it a reader (artist memory history <id>), or delete
  it. I lean delete — the event log already answers every question it could.

  Why this isn't grafted on

  The strongest argument that it's idiomatic isn't from database theory, it's
  from your own codebase.

  artist-session is already event log + rebuildable projections — build_history,
  render_markdown, replay_for_ui, rewind-as-masking. Memory being an event log
  plus an indexed projection is the same pattern the repo already runs on. The
  live flag is just that projection materializing "not superseded" in a form the
  index can use.

  If I'd made the searchable table itself temporal and filtered in Rust
  afterward — that would have been the bolt-on. Fighting the storage engine in
  application code is the tell. We're not doing that; the engine maintains the
  filter itself, inside the index, for free.

  So: the boolean isn't a consolation prize for missing time travel. It's what
  the correct design looks like, and the time travel was the thing that would
  have been out of place.

  Want me to cut fact_history, or give it that history subcommand?

● Updated plan

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Bolt-on removed. It turned out to be three things, not one.

  What came out

  fact_history — the TxTime audit sibling. It could carry no index, had no
  reader, and duplicated what memory.written already records with strictly more
  fidelity (origin, session, sequence). Every supersession was paying a write
  for it. supersede is now a single statement instead of a two-statement
  transaction.

  rel — an empty relation with no writer. Pure schema decoration.

  The graph-algo feature — enabled solely for BudgetedTraversal, which nothing
  calls. Dropping it removes the graph and graph_builder crates from the
  dependency tree entirely. rayon stays, but now only via rten, which genuinely
  needs it for inference parallelism.

  That last one also turned a researched claim into a verified one: the tests
  still pass, which proves ReciprocalRankFusion and MaximalMarginalRelevance
  really are registered unconditionally rather than behind that feature. Fused
  retrieval is unaffected.

  What's left, and why it's the right shape

  fact + chunk + meta. Supersession is a live flag with indexes filtered on it,
  and the event log is the system of record.

  I rewrote the doc comment and the architecture section to say that plainly,
  because the previous framing was subtly wrong. It read as though live were a
  consolation prize for the TxTime limitation. It isn't — it's SCD2 with a
  partial index, and it's the same event-log-plus-projection pattern the rest of
  the harness already runs on. The temporal sibling was the thing that didn't
  belong.

  Tests: 17 in artist-memory (plus 3 model-gated), 125 in artist-agent, 66 in 
  artist-session, and artist memory verify still opens both stores clean on a
  fresh config root.

  One consequence worth knowing: a dev store created before this change will
  still have orphaned fact_history and rel relations sitting in it. They're
  inert — apply_schema only creates what's missing and never touches what it
  doesn't know about, and neither appears in the export set. If you'd rather
  they be actively dropped on open, that's a small addition; I left it out
  because a schema-version bump for a subsystem that hasn't shipped seemed like
  more ceremony than the problem deserves.

● All three answered.

  1. The four shells were mine, and they were garbage

  Zombie polling loops — until [ -f /tmp/nonexistent-sentinel-N ]; do sleep 20; 
  done — left over from early in the session when I was waiting on the research
  agents. They'd been spinning for 2.2 to 2.7 hours on sentinel files that were
  never going to exist.

  Near-zero CPU (a sleep loop is just a timer), but they were holding shell
  slots and would have outlived the session. Killed, all four, confirmed gone.
  My mistake for using file-sentinel waits instead of just ending the turn and
  letting the task notifications wake me — which is what I switched to later.

  2. Memory is built, not switched on

  Honest status:

  Done and tested — the store, both scopes, fused retrieval, supersession,
  dedupe, export/import, rewind-awareness, all four write paths, all three read
  channels, settings, and the CLI subcommands. 17 tests in artist-memory, 131 in
  artist-agent. The embedder is verified against the real model, including a
  genuine semantic retrieval.

  Not done — three things, and one of them matters:

  - No embedding model is installed, and [memory] enabled defaults to false.
  Turning it on today means putting model.onnx + tokenizer.json at
  ~/.config/artist/models/code-embed/ yourself. I verified that path is empty.
  - The code index is unproven at scale. Wired, unit-tested, defaults off — but
  no full pass has run, and at measured throughput that's ~1h45m for 40k chunks.
  - Indexing isn't really incremental yet. The content-hash comparison works,
  but it fires once at session start rather than reacting to the fff-search
  watcher that already exists. That's the obvious next step.

  So: the facts half is genuinely usable the moment you drop a model in. The
  code-index half is scaffolding that hasn't met a real repository.

  3. The artifact

  Published. I built the diagrams in CSS rather than Mermaid so both themes are
  controlled precisely, and derived the palette from the subject — teal is the 
  vector leg, rust is the lexical leg, the two inks that merge in the fusion
  diagram.

  Five figures: the stack with the event log as its spine, the write triggers,
  the read channels, how a lookup resolves, and supersession. The fusion figure
  uses real RRF arithmetic and shows the thing that makes fusion worth having —
  fact 2 wins the merged ranking without topping either individual list.

  The status section is on the page too, so it doesn't read as a victory lap.

✻ Sautéed for 4m 36s

❯ One thing another dev proposed to me is this---since Cozo is a relational DB  
  and datalog makes this very effective, we could potentially enforce typed     
  ontology over memories and create a relations system. Is this an idea that    
  has water, or not really?                                                     

● Let me check something first, because it bears directly on the answer.

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

● Your dev has a point, and there's uncomfortable evidence for it sitting in the
  code right now: every write path passes "" for subject and predicate. All
  three of them. The SPO triple structure is in the schema and is 100% dead
  weight — which is exactly the bolt-on smell I just cleaned up twice and missed
  a third time.

  So the schema is already gesturing at an ontology and getting none of the
  benefit. That has to resolve one way or the other.

  My verdict: the idea has water, but the strong version is a well-documented 
  trap and the weak version is genuinely high-value. The gap between them is
  large.

  The strongest argument for it

  It retroactively justifies the engine choice. I said earlier that when
  bitemporality turned out to be unusable, it "removed most of Mnestic's
  specific advantage." That's still true. If memory is prose blobs plus vector
  search, you didn't need a Datalog engine — SQLite with FTS5 and a vector
  column would have done it with a fraction of the dependency weight and no GCC
  workaround.

  Relations are the thing that makes Cozo the right call rather than an
  expensive one. Right now it isn't earning its keep.

  What you genuinely cannot answer today

  Two, and they're not academic:

  Automatic supersession. Today, correcting a fact requires the model to notice
  the old one exists, recall its id, and call supersede. That's fragile — it'll
  silently fail most of the time and you'll accumulate contradictory facts. With
  a small set of functional predicates (prefers, forbids, requires), a
  collision on (subject, predicate) is a correction, detectable by the database.
  You'd learn (user, prefers-editor, X), later learn (user, prefers-editor, Y),
  and supersession happens without the model participating at all.

  That's the killer app. It fixes a real weakness I shipped.

  Entity-anchored invalidation. A memory about should_compact is stale when that
  function is deleted. A prose blob cannot know this. An edge from fact →
  path/symbol gives you both garbage collection and a recall boost when you're
  working in that file. Cheap to populate, immediately useful.

  Where typed ontologies actually die

  Not in the schema — the schema is the easy part. They die in consistent 
  population, and the populator here is an LLM.

  You will get prefers, prefer, likes, wants, and favours as five predicates
  meaning one thing. This is the failure mode that killed Freebase and a
  thousand enterprise knowledge graphs, and an LLM is more prone to it than a
  human data-entry team, not less.

  The fork is unforgiving:
  - Closed vocabulary — the model constantly hits facts that don't fit, and
  either force-fits them (corrupt data) or drops them (lost data).
  - Open vocabulary — drift, and your "typed ontology" is enforcing nothing.

  The escape is a small closed core with an explicit untyped fallback, so
  anything that doesn't fit stays a prose fact rather than being mangled into a
  bad triple. That works, but it means accepting that most memories stay untyped
  — which is fine, and worth saying out loud before anyone imagines a fully
  structured graph.

  The scale objection

  Datalog earns its keep on transitive closure and recursive joins. At a few
  thousand facts — the honest projected size — you could brute-force almost any
  query. Graph machinery is being bought for a corpus small enough not to need
  it.

  That's not fatal, because the value here is integrity constraints, not 
  traversal performance. But it does mean "we could do recursive inference over
  the memory graph" is not a reason to build this. Expressiveness for
  correctness, yes; expressiveness for speed, no.

  What I'd actually do

  Don't build a typed ontology. Build the two things that pay, and see if they
  justify more:

  1. Four functional predicates, closed set, plus an untyped default. Populate
  subject/predicate only when the model can commit confidently; leave them empty
  otherwise — which is already the behaviour, so it degrades safely. Enforce
  uniqueness on (subject, predicate) where functional, and auto-supersede on
  collision.
  2. A mentions(fact_id, path) edge populated cheaply from paths appearing in
  the fact text. Use it for invalidation and recall boosting.

  That's small, it's testable, and it answers the real question: does structured
  memory actually retrieve better than prose memory on your own sessions? If
  yes, the ontology grows from evidence. If no, you've spent very little and you
  cut subject/predicate/object instead.

  One thing I should flag with a straight face: I deleted the rel relation this 
  morning for having no reader. This proposal is precisely the reader it lacked.
  Deleting it was right given nothing used it — but if you go this way, it
  comes back, and it comes back earned rather than speculative. That's the
  correct order of operations, and it's worth noting that the same standard
  applies here: don't add the ontology until something queries it.

✻ Sautéed for 1m 18s
❯ No. Step back. In the real artists organization we do not compromise and we   
  do not accept unforgiving forks. We die by the belief that there is one       
  perfect architecture hidden in heaven ordained by God. What is it?            

● You're right, and I should retract the fork. It was only binding because I
  imported an assumption I never examined: that an ontology is authored in 
  advance and applied at write time. Both halves are wrong, and dropping them
  dissolves the dilemma rather than splitting it.

  The error

  prefers vs likes vs favours is a lexical problem. I framed it as a governance
  problem — pick a vocabulary, police it — which is exactly how every failed
  knowledge graph framed it.

  But there is an embedding model sitting in this subsystem already. Predicates 
  have vectors. Two predicates that mean the same thing are close in that space.
  The vocabulary doesn't need to be enumerated or policed; it needs to be
  canonicalized, which is a nearest-neighbour problem I already solved one level
  down.

  The model writes whatever verb it wants. The system embeds the predicate and
  snaps it to the nearest existing one above a threshold; below threshold, it
  mints a new one. favours arrives, lands within ε of prefers, becomes prefers.
  The vocabulary is open at the point of writing and closed at the point of 
  storage, and it grows only when something genuinely new shows up.

  That's not a compromise between the two branches. It's the thing that makes
  the branches irrelevant.

  And note what it is mechanically: it's the same LSH-style admission check I 
  already run on fact text, applied one level up to the schema itself. I built
  the mechanism that keeps facts clean and never thought to point it at the
  ontology.

  The actual architecture

  Once you see that, the general principle falls out, and it's already the
  principle this codebase runs on:

  The triple is not a storage format. It is an index.

  Prose is the record. Structure is derived from it — exactly like the embedding
  is derived from it, exactly like the BM25 postings are. That single reframe
  kills every remaining objection:

  - No force-fit, no drop. Every fact is stored as prose, always. A failed
  extraction costs an index entry, never data.
  - Bad extraction is repairable. When the extractor improves, rebuild the
  triple index from the prose. That is impossible if triples are the storage
  format — you'd have destroyed the source.
  - Drift is fixable retroactively, not merely preventable. Re-cluster the whole
  predicate space periodically and merge; nothing is lost because nothing was
  ever stored as a predicate.

  So it's projections all the way down. events.jsonl is truth and the database
  is a projection — and within the database, prose is truth and embedding,
  postings, shingles, triples, and entity links are all projections of that. The
  same pattern, one level of recursion deeper. That self-similarity is the tell
  that it's right.

  The part that goes further than your dev proposed

  Don't declare which predicates are functional either. Observe it.

  If (user, prefers-editor, *) has historically held exactly one live value, and
  each new one has superseded the last, then it is functional — empirically,
  from the data. Cozo can express that as a rule over the fact table. You never
  write down a constraint; you compute which constraints hold and enforce the
  ones that do.

  Same move as the vocabulary: derive, don't declare. That's the invariant the
  whole design turns on.

  Which means the layering is:

  Prose — verbatim, never lossy, the unit of meaning.
  Derived indexes — embedding, postings, shingles, triples, entity links. All
  rebuildable, all optional per row, none authoritative.
  Derived constraints — functionality, contradiction, staleness. Computed from
  the index layer, never authored.

  Nothing in that has a fork in it. There is no branch where you accept
  corruption or accept loss.

  What it honestly costs

  Not compromises, but real engineering: extraction is a cost per write or a
  batch pass; the canonicalization threshold needs tuning against your own
  corpus; and derived functionality needs enough observations before it's
  trustworthy, so there's a cold-start period where it abstains.

  Those are problems with answers. That's the difference between a cost and a
  compromise — and it's the distinction I blurred by presenting the fork at all.

  Want me to build it? The first increment is small and self-proving: embed
  predicates, canonicalize on write, and let supersession fall out of a derived
  functional constraint rather than the model remembering to call supersede. It
  uses the embedder and index that already exist, and it earns rel back
  properly.

● Good — scoping it properly surfaces something the verbal answer glossed over.
  Let me build it.

  Design plan — this is a proposal, not a description, so it gets a sibling
  identity rather than the explainer's: the two inks now encode the load-bearing
  distinction, #2F4A7A indigo for declared (fixed, structural, ours) against
  #4A7C4E botanical green for derived (emergent, grown from the corpus). Same
  serif/sans/mono trio for family resemblance. Layout is type-table dominant,
  since a type system is what's being scoped.

● Artifact(/tmp/claude-1000/-home-adam-Projects-artist/e3e3e41a-ca4e-4c6b-814f-5
          0cec4a3bc65/scratchpad/memory-ontology.html)
  ⎿  Published ⧉ 
     https://claude.ai/code/artifact/487bf2b1-d07a-44a4-88cd-ea1b8d0d8206

● Published.

  The answer to "what types"

  Five declared enums, seventeen variants. That's the entire fixed surface:

  ┌─────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │             │                                                            │
  ├─────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Referent    │ Resolvable / Opaque / Literal — can this be checked        │
  │             │ against the world?                                         │
  ├─────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Cardinality │ Functional / MultiValued / Accretive / Unknown — is a      │
  │             │ collision a correction?                                    │
  ├─────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Provenance  │ Stated / Observed / Inferred — who wins a conflict?        │
  ├─────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Polarity    │ Affirm / Negate                                            │
  ├─────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ EdgeKind    │ Supersedes / Contradicts / Supports / Mentions             │
  └─────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The generating rule, which is what actually makes the list short: a type is 
  worth declaring only if the system branches on it. If the answer changes what
  code runs, declare it. If it only changes what the fact means, derive it.
  That's why there's no entity taxonomy, no domain hierarchy, and no predicate
  list — those are precisely the parts that rot.

  What scoping it surfaced that the verbal answer missed

  Embeddings place antonyms close together. "prefers" and "dislikes" share
  nearly every contextual feature except sign. A naive nearest-neighbour
  canonicalizer merges them, and the system then confidently recalls the exact
  inverse of your stated preference.

  That's not a tuning problem, it's a category error — and it's the one place
  where "derive, don't declare" produces a catastrophic rather than a merely
  untidy failure. So Polarity gets declared, and canonicalization runs within a
  polarity class, never across it. One axis written down, for a specific and
  demonstrable reason.

  I'd rather have found that now than after shipping it.

  Two other things that fell out

  Cardinality can be revised, not just concluded. A single counter-example
  demotes Functional to MultiValued and reinstates what was wrongly retired —
  which only works because supersession flips live rather than deleting. The
  existing design already supports the undo this needs, which is a good sign the
  two fit.

  Unknown is the default and the system abstains. Cold start is silent rather
  than wrong. Below the observation threshold nothing is auto-superseded at all.

  What I did not resolve

  Four open questions on the page, and the honest one is the last:
  canonicalizing predicates is the easy half. Deciding that "the user", "Adam"
  and "you" are one subject is the same mechanism against a far larger space,
  and I don't think embedding-plus-threshold is sufficient there. That needs its
  own answer before this is real.

  The standing constraint is on the page too: nothing gets built until something
  queries it. The capability table names what would consume each type — each
  one earns its place when that consumer is implemented, not before.

✻ Brewed for 3m 19s

❯ Whats the optionspace for that answer? Also, how are types assigned?          

● Two good questions, and the first one has a better answer than I gave — I
  inherited my own framing without checking it.

  1. Subject identity — the optionspace

  I said it was "the same mechanism against a far larger space." That's wrong
  twice over.

  Embedding similarity is the wrong tool here, sometimes an anti-signal.
  compaction.rs and handoff.rs sit close in embedding space — both Rust, both
  about context management — and are emphatically not the same subject.
  Meanwhile foo and crate::tools::foo are lexically distant and are the same
  subject. For predicates, similarity tracks identity. For subjects, it actively
  doesn't.

  And the space isn't uniformly large. It splits — and it splits along Referent,
  the enum already declared. The type system answers its own hardest question:

  Resolvable → ground it, don't cluster it. A path, symbol, branch or crate has
  a canonical identity available by lookup. Two mentions are the same subject
  iff they resolve to the same artifact. Exact, verifiable, zero drift — and
  it's the large space, which is fine precisely because you never have to guess.
  This also yields invalidation for free, which was already on the wanted list.

  Opaque → a tiny declared set. The opaque subjects that carry functional
  predicates are deictic: the user, the project, the session, the agent. That's
  closed for a structural reason — they're the participants in the conversation,
  not arbitrary entities. Four variants, written down.

  Literal → no identity needed. Terminal, objects only.

  So the unbounded-space fear was misplaced: the large space is exactly the one
  with exact resolution, and the fuzzy space is small enough to enumerate.

  The fallback ladder for anything that escapes both: exact string match → an
  alias edge recorded when a correction reveals two names are one thing →
  abstain. Learn identity from corrections rather than guessing at it. Never
  wrong, only incomplete.

  Two options I'd actively reject: LLM coreference at write time (expensive,
  inconsistent, reintroduces exactly the drift the canonicalization design
  exists to prevent), and embedding clustering on subjects (for the anti-signal
  reason above — it would merge sibling files and split renamed symbols).

  2. How types are assigned

  The governing fact is the one from the artifact: a triple is an index, not a 
  storage format. So assignment can be deferred, retried, and rebuilt — which
  means it does not have to happen at write time and does not have to succeed.

  There is no single assigner. Each type comes from the cheapest mechanism that
  can produce it:

  ┌──────────────┬──────────────────────────┬────────────────┬──────────────┐
  │     Type     │       Assigned by        │      When      │  On failure  │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Provenance   │ the harness — which      │ write,         │ can't fail   │
  │              │ write path fired         │ deterministic  │              │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Polarity     │ negation detection over  │ write,         │ → Affirm     │
  │              │ the predicate span       │ deterministic  │              │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │              │ resolver — does it       │                │              │
  │ Referent     │ resolve to a real        │ async          │ → Opaque     │
  │              │ path/symbol/branch?      │                │              │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ predicate    │ embed + canonicalize     │ async          │ → mint new   │
  │ identity     │                          │                │              │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ subject /    │ extraction               │ batch          │ → fact stays │
  │ object       │                          │                │  prose-only  │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ EdgeKind     │ extraction + resolution  │ batch          │ → absent     │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Cardinality  │ observation across the   │ periodic       │ → Unknown,   │
  │              │ corpus                   │                │ abstain      │
  └──────────────┴──────────────────────────┴────────────────┴──────────────┘

  Two things worth pulling out of that table.

  Provenance is free, and it's the most authoritative type. The harness already
  knows whether a fact came from the tool, a correction, model output, or a
  commit — that's the origin field shipping today. The type that decides who
  wins a conflict costs nothing to assign.

  Extraction should be a batch sweep, and not primarily for cost. The obvious
  options are widening the memory tool schema so the model supplies structure
  inline, or a second call per write. But three of the four write paths have no 
  model in the loop at that moment — corrections are stored verbatim by design,
  commits are detected from a tool result, output-triggered writes fire
  mid-stream. Inline structure only ever covers the deliberate tool path.

  A sweep over untyped facts — at session end or on idle — is the only mechanism
  that covers all four uniformly. It's also nearly free given the volume: facts
  accrue at a handful per session, not thousands.

  Every row degrades to something safe, and no row blocks a write. A fact with
  no extracted structure is exactly what ships today: prose, embedded,
  searchable. The types are strictly additive, which is what makes the whole
  thing safe to build incrementally — and what makes a bad extractor a fixable
  mistake rather than a corrupt corpus.

  The one thing I'd want measured before committing: whether Referent resolution
  is cheap enough to run async per fact, or whether it also belongs in the
  sweep. That depends on how often memories name things that need a filesystem
  or git lookup, which I don't know yet.
